**2 DONE (2026-10-16, branch `s27/tools` off `s27/finish` 75e13fe, not merged; typecheck + 1150 tests green — the trace walls.test 3 s time check fails now and then while three Firefoxes run, passes alone; commits caeb971, 1b51e03 + this one).** His three Studio asks, one reducer (`studio/model.ts`), every drag one Ctrl+Z (drag-begin holds the entry). **(a) Column = four corner points:** `Pillar.corners?` (core type extended, optional; `core.pillarCorners(p)` gives the four points, the rectangle when absent; x / y / wM / hM stay its bounding box). A picked column shows 8 dots (4 corners, 4 side middles). A corner dot moves THAT corner alone (`pillar-corner`) — a tapered / wedge column, straight sides; its two sides stick when within 3° of square or of parallel to the side across (`stickCorner`; "a bit sticky, not too much": a 2° pull snaps back to the plain rectangle, 6° stays; Shift or a snap to a wall corner turns it off); a bow tie or a dent is refused. A side dot moves that side across, whole inches (rectangles as before). Typed Width / Depth stay: on a tapered column they show the outer size and stretch it about its middle (the smaller change than disabling them; the panel says so). 3D (`three/details.ts pillarParts`) extrudes the four points (faces, top, skirting per side); Studio snapping uses the four corners and sides. **Left out:** rooms around a tapered column (`core/graph.ts` joins by its bounding box — `ponytail:` note there), the Building view's massing and the 3D's passage-trim test still use the box; no key for "make it square again" (drag a corner back — it sticks — or Ctrl+Z). **(b) Wall thickness by dragging:** one picked wall (W or low wall; not a kerb / zone line) shows a dot on the middle of each long face; dragging one (`wall-face`) sets the thickness to the pointer's distance from the OTHER face, whole inches, 1" at least — that face stays, the centre line shifts by half the change. The straight run it belongs to (pieces split by T-joints, same line, same thickness) changes with it, so a run never kinks; the walls at its corners follow as at a dragged corner (their ends slide). Typed Width field unchanged. **(c) A = Arc** (A was free): click start, click end (both snap like a W corner: corners, wall lines — a wall is split there — columns), move to bend (a circle through start, end and the pointer, previewed with "N straight walls" in the status bar), click (or press-drag-release) to lay; Esc cancels. `add-arc` lays straight walls ≤ 10° of turn each, none under 0.3 m (a tight arc gets fewer, longer pieces; a bulge under 1 cm = one straight wall), joined end to end, the W tool's type, 5" thick; its ends join as a W corner (`settle`), so an arc from wall to wall closes a bay R can name; one undo; the new pieces (only they) are picked — T / the Width box / Del act on all. Rooms, doors, snapping, 3D and the tracer stay straight-wall only (`ponytail:` true arcs later if ever). **Proof as the founder (Firefox, `E:/dev/tmp/s27/tools/proof.mjs`, shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s27/tools/01…11`):** BTI sheet dropped, S on 600 px = 34', a room with W, a column with C; 02 the 8 dots; 03 the bottom-right corner dragged out → tapered (panel "tapered: Width and Depth are its outer size"); pulled back to 2° it stuck to the rectangle, Ctrl+Z gave the taper back; 04 / 05 the top wall's face dots, the outer one dragged up 5": 5" → 10", inside face unchanged to 0.1 mm; 06 the arc preview ("10 straight walls"), 07 laid and picked (10 selected), 08 R named the bay "Bay" and the room "Living" (both closed); 09–11 Preview 3D: the tapered column with its slanted face and skirting, seen from above, the room with its curved bay. Tests (`studio.test.ts`, "Studio tools (session 27 …)"): a corner move keeps the other three corners + sticks + undo + side + typed stretch; a face drag keeps the other face and moves the run and its T stem; an arc lays 18 joined pieces ≤ 10° on a half circle, a wall-to-wall bay closes a room and picks only its pieces.

**3 DONE (2026-10-15, branch `s27/aiprep` off `s27/finish`, not merged):** a vision model later only answers rows; none is plugged in. (a) Every open Check-these row in `state.review.items` now carries `box` {x,y,w,h} (sheet image px: its spot ± 2.5 m at least, its entity and every answer's ghost, 1 m around, clipped to the picture) and `answers` [{id, label, title, actions}] = exactly its buttons (its fixes, its twin issue's, Show me, Looks right; id = the label dashed, e.g. `close-it-zone-line`, `make-it-a-wall`, `looks-right`, `show-me`). Worked out by `issues.ts rowAnswers(unit, marks, fixes, size)` at the end of StudioApp's fixes loop (the loop now keys on the marks' keys, so storing answers never starts a second round) and stored by the `answers` action (ignored if the unit changed meanwhile); any edit drops them (`review.ts unanswered`) until the loop re-works them, so stored answers always match the stored plan. The actions are plain reducer actions (JSON), no closures. Saved with the draft (`plotline.studio.draft`, as `review` already was) and in the Export file (`review` beside the unit; Import strips it). (b) `text.ts readText` keeps `TextTrace.rejected`: {box image px, angle 0/90/-90 = the clockwise turn that reads it upright, reason `garbage` (a room word the nameOk gate refused) / `size-unread` (a label's size line unsure or missing) / `name-unread` (the strip over a lone size no name came from), text}; `trace/index.ts` hands it to every pick as `AutoTraceResult.rejectedText`; the Studio stores it in `plotline.studio.strips` = {"<file> <w>x<h>": [...]}, boxes only. `text.ts uprightStrip(gray, strip)` → the crop at full size turned upright (`toCanvas` for a canvas). (c) `review.ts applyAnswer(state, rowId, answerId)` = `studioReducer` with `{type:'answer'}`: a fix goes through `apply-fix` (one undo step, Ctrl+Z as before), Looks right = `dismiss-review`, Show me = the Wall tool; an answer the row no longer has changes nothing. The Panel row buttons, Show me, Looks right and the on-plan popover's buttons for a row all dispatch it (StudioApp `answer`); Issues rows keep `apply-fix`. (d) `review.ts logAnswer(localStorage, state, rowId, answerId)` appends {sheet {name,w,h}, rowId, kind, row (its text on screen), answerId, answer, time ISO} to `plotline.answers` (read, append, write; never edits; a corrupt log is left alone); top bar **Export answers** downloads it as `plotline-answers.json`. **What a model will do:** read the draft's open rows (those with `answers`), show it the sheet crop `box` (and the `plotline.studio.strips` crops via `uprightStrip`), get back one answer id per row, call `applyAnswer`; score it against `plotline.answers`. **Proof** (Firefox headless, Playwright's E: build, script `E:/dev/tmp/s27/aiprep/proof.mjs`, shots + log `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s27/aiprep/`): bti Amabilia Type A, S on BED-1 16'-0", Auto-trace Whole floor → 52 rows on screen, 52 in the saved draft, all 52 with box + answers; "Close it — zone line" on Ac: walls 105 → 107, rows 52 → 51, toast "… Ctrl+Z undoes it"; Ctrl+Z: 105 walls, 52 rows; again: 107 / 51; Looks right on another row: 50; Export answers: 3 entries (two Close it, one Looks right) with sheet "Type A 2279 sft.jpg 2560x1813"; Export carries 51 rows; reload: 50 rows on screen, boxes identical to before (`localStorage-after-reload.json`), console clean. Strip crops from `uprightStrip` (`strips.mjs`): Amabilia 15 size-unread; Finlay Palm Spring A 6 (4 turned, e.g. a sideways LIVING 13'-0"X11'-0" saved upright), Assure Phera 9; row-box crops `rowbox-*.png`. Tests: issues.test.ts (row answers + box, draft round trip, applyAnswer = the old button, undo, append-only log), textPasses.test.ts (uprightStrip pure; size-unread strips with angles 0 / -90 / 90 on the sideways sheet; a garbage strip). **Left out:** typing a name (Name it) is not logged as an answer (free text); the Issues list's own fixes are not answers; Import does not bring an Export's rows back; no pixels are stored (crops are cut from the picture when needed); a row shows answers only once its fixes are worked out (as its buttons do); rows hidden as done keep no answers.

**4 FIX (2026-10-18, branch `s27/fix` off `s27/finish` d24ad94; one agent; commits 8e7a84e (the rules) + this one (HANDOFF / MANUAL, the glass row's wording); typecheck + `npx vitest run src/trace src/studio src/core` green — 840 passed, 9 skipped on 8e7a84e; on this commit 839 passed + walls.test.ts's 3-second check, which failed in the full run while the test-lane Firefoxes loaded the machine and passes alone (walls.ts's tracing untouched by these rules but for one flag)).** The fix job after REVIEW-1 (`E:/dev/plotline-shots/s27/run1/REVIEW-1.md`), causes 1, 3 and 7 in that order; four general rules, each with its test beside its module (each fails without its rule). **(1) A window drawn as a frame band** (`tracks.ts classifyGap`, cause 1): a light band as wide as the wall with ONE dark pane line in it (the photo-style brochures: Akter Residence Park) between two wall ends is a window in that wall, as `walls.ts` and `close.ts` already counted a glass band — before, the tracks tracer took only two or three separate lines, so the corner bedrooms behind long windows stayed open (Residence Park p33 Child / Guest Bed tops). Only where the picture is traced at its own size (`TrackOpts.frameBands`, off inside `traceWalls`' blown-up retrace of a small picture): on Magnifico, Water Lily, Kanakchapa and La Reina (walls under 6 px, traced at 2×) a thin wall's own fill blurs into such a band and flipped dozens of 0.3–0.6 m gaps to windows (Magnifico −2, Water Lily +5 false rooms) — measured, then gated. **(2) A window drawn as one line** (`close.ts` sideKind, cause 1, founder 2026-10-09 "non-wall edges"): a loose wall end carried along its OWN line to the wall ahead with ONE thin line drawn along it all the way is a glass wall (before: a low wall on a veranda, else a zone line — refused for a bedroom), kept only through the same gate (the room closes alone at its print, or no print and ≤ 3 guesses and its kind's size). Its Check-these row (`solve.ts closeMessage`) now says "a glass wall … along the window line drawn there" for one line or two (it said "along the double line"). **(3) A dashed or dash-dot line is never a wall** (`raster.ts dashMask`, cause 3): dark runs 0.15–1.5 m long and ≤ 0.35 m thick, five or more dashes in a row of about one length (≤ 1.6×), gaps ≤ 0.5 m, a dash-dot line's dots between them any length — left out with the furniture before tracing (`solve.ts prepareTraces`) and out of `outlinedWalls`' "long solid walls" count. On Akter Anandita the site's dash-dot plot line (1.45 m dashes) counted as the sheet's solid walls (37 397 px against 46 593 outlined), so the hollow-wall reader never fired and the whole site traced as one face; now it fires (p6 1 → 14 faces, p12 1 → 13 in the wall stage alone). **(4) A guessed kind only where the room can be one** (`solve.ts pickTraces` roomy, cause 7): a kind from the fill colour of named rooms or from a drawn fixture is given only when the room is at least the kind's narrowest width squared (`CLOSE.narrowM`: bed 2.1 m → 4.4 m²). The sheet (grey and colour) is turned square into a canvas that holds it all, read and traced as any sheet, and every pick / hover is turned back (vertices, names, columns as four corners, rows, printed names, fixtures, the rejected strips' boxes). **SITE + WIND DONE (2026-10-18, branch `s27/site` off `s27/finish` 9640d9b, worktree `E:/dev/Plotline/.claude/worktrees/s27-site`, not merged; commits 8c2e725, de9e1bf, 67cc89d, c9f0632, 66b115b, 84ee8f0 + this one; `npm run typecheck` green; `npm test` 1175 passed, 1 failed = `trace/walls.test.ts`'s eval timing under load, which passes alone (9 of 9), as before; `src/core/site.test.ts` 17 passed). Firefox proof as the founder done — `E:/dev/tmp/s27/site/run.mjs` (PORT 5622, one browser, 85 s, 0 console errors), shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s27/site/01…12`: Studio `/studio?unit=type-a` → Site → "Banani, Dhaka" (Nominatim found it) → click the middle of the map → "509 buildings and 39 roads within 250 m, the wind by month" (Overpass answered 502 without CORS; the OpenStreetMap box read took over) → True north typed 0, then the arrow dragged up-left onto the sheet's own N arrow = 305° (the hand-set value was 304°) → the nearest block west typed 40 m → Preview 3D → Dollhouse with the grey blocks, roads and their shadows → 21 June, 15:00 → Wind: "June · SSE · 3.5 m/s … Cross ventilation: Bed-2, Living room, Bed-1 … Open-Meteo, 2016–2025" → Living room chip "Cross ventilation: the wind comes in and goes out" → Building view. Found and fixed by the proof: a map building overlapping the drawing filled Type A's rooms to 2.4 m (a 9 m block from the street) — `core.sitePlan` now leaves out any neighbour within 1 m of the drawing's box (the Studio map draws it dotted, "left out of the 3D"); the first streamlines (pale blue, 0.6) were invisible on the light rooms — now slate `#4a6f94` at 0.9, read in the walk shot as fine drifting pen lines; the rose's second line named "south" beside its "SSE" — now says steady or not. Shot 03's map: OpenStreetMap's own grey buildings sit under the overlay, so the overlay mostly shows when one is clicked (red). Polish pass on his "classy" note (a596f31, re-shot in one more run, 83 s, 0 errors): the rose 180 px with rings / ticks a step clearer, spokes 1.5 px, the picked month 2.75 px and brightest, its card above the time strip and the walk hint (bottom 112 px); the streamlines are three's screen-width `LineSegments2` at 2 px (× pixel ratio), the same fades and slow soft dashes, slate `#46709a` at 0.95 — in `11-wind-june-walk-living.png` they read as soft pen lines from the sliding door across to the far windows.** His extra items 2 and 3 ("a wind simulator … the direction of the flat is normally given on the floor plan"; "maps … the surrounding buildings and the roads nearby, for realistic wind and light"; "no cost, I am broke"). **Map source decision:** Google Maps needs a billing account with a card even for its free credit — out. OpenStreetMap only, no key, no account, no library (no Leaflet / MapLibre): the Studio shows a 3 × 3 grid of plain `<img>` tiles from tile.openstreetmap.org at zoom 18 (9 requests per opening, attribution under it); an address goes to Nominatim (one request per Find; "lat, lon" pasted needs none); buildings + roads within 250 m come from Overpass (overpass-api.de, 20 s), and when it does not answer — it refused or timed out from this machine most of 2026-10-18 night, the public mirrors gave 500 / 504 — from OpenStreetMap's own `map.json` box read (one request, 500 m box, same reader: `core.waysFromOsmMap`); the wind from Open-Meteo's archive (daily mean speed + dominant direction at 10 m, the ten full years before the fetch, vector-averaged per month: `core.windFromDaily`; its `utc_offset_seconds` is the site's clock). Everything is fetched ONCE in the Studio and saved on the drawing (`Unit.site`, and on every drawing of the open project): Preview 3D, the Building view and buyer links never fetch. **What was built (smallest diff):** `core/types.ts` `Unit.site?: Site` {lat, lon, utcOffsetH?, fetchedAt?, buildings [{id, pts (m, x east / y south of the plot), heightM, from: height / levels / guess / typed}], roads [{id, pts, widthM, name?}], wind? [12 × {fromDeg, speedMs, steady}]}; true north stays `Unit.northDeg` (the one north the sun, minimap, compass and wall names read — not duplicated into the site). `core/site.ts`: `sunPosition` / `sunAt` (NOAA; Dhaka 21 June 12:00 = 89.6°, 21 December 12:00 = 42.8°, tested), `siteFromOverpass` (height tag, else `building:levels` × 3 m, else 9 m guessed; a height under 2 or over 6 m a floor of its levels is a typo → levels — Banani's map has 60 m on 5 floors; a footprint round the plot point is the old house: dropped; vehicle roads only, width tag → lanes × 3.5 → by class), `siteToPlan` (the plot point = the drawing's centre, turned by northDeg), `roomWind` (outside openings — the other side no room, a balcony or a zone — within 60° of the wind take it in, those facing ≥ 90° away let it out, both = cross ventilation; doors to the next room do not count), `sheltered` (a block within 30 m upwind, its top above floor × 3.2 + 0.2 m: dimmed), `DHAKA_WIND` (Open-Meteo 2015–2024 at 23.79 N 90.40 E, fetched 2026-10-18: NW in Nov–Feb, S / SE in Apr–Sep, March and October no steady direction). Studio: top bar **Site** (`studio/SitePanel.tsx`): Find, the map, click the plot (asks before moving an existing one), blocks / roads drawn on the map (dashed = height guessed, gold = the drawing turned by its north), click one → type its height / Delete; True north typed, or the gold **north arrow** dragged on the plan (its dot moves it onto the sheet's arrow). 3D: `three/context.ts buildSite` — plain grey extruded blocks (cast + receive shadow) and dark road strips, in Walk, Dollhouse and Building (Look puts them in its always-visible group; the Building view draws its own and skips the tower's authored roads); the shadow box reaches 300 m toward the sun (`render.ts SITE_REACH_M`, the sun placed 300 m further out). The sun: `PlotlineScene.setTimeOfDay(hour, date)` = `core.sunAt(site ?? Dhaka, date, hour)` by northDeg (was one equinox path at Dhaka); the viewer's sun strip got a date field (starts on today) and a **Wind** button: `SunPill.tsx WindRose` (redone on his note "be classy about it", commit c9f0632: thin rings 1 m/s apart, 16 ticks, N E S W in a light serif, one slim spoke per month in one quiet blue, length = speed, the date's month deeper with "June · SSE · 3.5 m/s"; a click picks a month), `three/wind.ts WindFlow` (6–10 thin curved streamlines per opening, one LineSegments with a small shader: fade in at the window, out past the far opening, long soft dashes drifting at 0.25–0.45 m/s, one desaturated blue at 0.6 opacity, 0.35 of that when sheltered; no arrowheads), the room chip says "Cross ventilation …". MANUAL §2 (sun strip, Wind, around the flat), §4, §5 (Site), §9, §10, §11 updated. **Real data / guesses:** the sun = formula (exact to well under 1°; the clock = Open-Meteo's offset, else round(lon / 15)); wind = real reanalysis (ERA5 via Open-Meteo), monthly means, NOT gusts, NOT the street-level wind between buildings; roads = real OSM centre lines, widths mostly guessed by class; building footprints = real OSM, heights mostly GUESSED (Banani: 396 of 407 have no height or floors → 9 m). **Left / not done:** (1) the founder's eye on the shots (the rose `10b-wind-rose-close.png`, the lines `11-wind-june-walk-living.png`); (2) Overpass from this machine refused / timed out all night — every fetch so far came from the fallback; (3) adding a block or road by hand; a hover tooltip on a block in 3D ("guessed" shows in the Studio map); (4) wind through doors into the next room; (5) a project's site sits at the centre of the flat being looked at, not the plot's outline; (6) roads past the 600 m ground plane float in the haze (invisible).

**SITE + WIND, ROUND 2 (2026-10-18 afternoon, branch `s27/site` after merging `s27/finish` a0e536c; commits 618c4f6 (sun tests), 12fd250 (shadow bias), 1dd0e66 (the wind inside the flat) + this one; typecheck green, `npm test` 1224 passed / 9 skipped).** His notes: "i cannot see the sunlight simulation … that bar is gone that i could move to see the sun through different times of the day"; "the small radar … should be optional … it blocks the view … show from where the wind is coming in and in which direction it is going out of the flat … seasonal effects". **The sun, measured:** `core.sunAt` 21 June 15:00 Dhaka = 49° up at 279° (UTC+6 applied the right way; read as UTC it would be under the horizon — tested); at the equinox it agrees with the old one-path sun within 3° from 8 to 17 h (tested), and the plan mapping (bearingInPlan, plan-up = world −Z) is the old N / E vectors. Type A's living room faces north-east (55°) and south-east (145°): the 15:00 June sun (west) cannot enter it, so shot 11 was right to show no patch — the morning sun does. Found on the way and fixed: the shadow bias is in the shadow map's depth units, and the site's 300 m reach had made the flat's −0.0005 ≈ 17 cm (shadows starting late at walls and sills); now held at 2 cm (`render.ts SHADOW_BIAS_M`), the tower keeps its own. Proof (Firefox, `E:/dev/tmp/s27/site/sun.mjs`, one browser, 101 s, 0 errors; shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s27/site/sun/`): the pre-site tree (worktree of 9640d9b — 3227624 already holds the site work) and this one, same Living room view: at 9:00 both show the sun patch on the sofa and floor (`old-livingroom-anyday-9h.png` / `new-livingroom-06-21-9h.png`), at 15:00 neither does (the windows face away), Bed-1 at 15:00 the same on both; December too (`new-livingroom-12-21-*`), and with the Banani site (`new-site-livingroom-06-21-*`). **The bar:** back as a time-of-day bar — a sun mark, a 320 px slider with "6 · morning · noon · afternoon · 18" under it, the day at its left end, Wind at its right end (`SunPill.tsx`). **Wind:** the button turns on the flow only; a thin line above the bar holds the twelve months (a click picks one) and the month in words with its season ("June · monsoon · steady south-south-east breeze, 3.5 m/s · hot and humid, about 28 °C and 85 % humidity" — `core.monthLine` / `seasonNote`: Bangladesh's four seasons in the monsoon belt 5–35° N 60–100° E, the temperate four elsewhere; Beaufort's words; hot ≥ 27 °C, warm ≥ 22, humid ≥ 80 %, dry < 65 %), and **Rose** (hidden by default, remembered in `plotline.windRose`; small, bottom left above Quality). Walking: chips over the room's openings, "Wind in · SSE" and "Out" (`WindChips`), and the room box in words (`core.windSentence`: "Wind comes in at the south window and goes out through the north window" / "… no way out: it stays in the room" / "No window or door here faces the wind"; the exit = the outlet farthest downwind, `RoomWind.exit`; a door onto a balcony is "the balcony door"). The lines bunch at the two openings and fan out between, dashes every 1.4 m, tinted by season (`wind.ts SEASON_TINT`: monsoon `#46709a`, winter `#7d8fa3`, the hot months `#b06f45`, between `#5c7286`). The site fetch now asks Open-Meteo for temperature and humidity too; the Dhaka table has 2015–2024's (Jan 18 °C 75 % … Jun 29 °C 84 % …); a site fetched before, near Dhaka, uses the Dhaka table for the note. Proof, same run (`E:/dev/plotline-shots/s27/site/wind-*.png`, Type A with the Banani site): Wind on, rose hidden; Living room: "Wind comes in at the south-east window and the south-east balcony door and goes out through the north-east window", chips Wind in · SSE ×2 / Out, the lines from the two in-chips to the out-chip (`wind-a-june-living.png`); Bed-2 June in SE / out NW (`wind-b-june-bed2.png`); January "winter · steady light north-west wind, 2 m/s · cool, about 20 °C and 73 % humidity", the flow turned round (`wind-c-january-bed2.png`); April "pre-monsoon summer · steady light south wind, 2.9 m/s · hot, about 27 °C and 73 % humidity" (`wind-d-april-bed2.png`); Rose shown (`wind-e-rose-shown.png`). Seen in the shots and fixed after (not re-shot): the rose overlapped the Quality menu when that showed its note (now 150 px up while it does), and April's warm stone lines read as scratches on the cream walls (now a muted terracotta `#b06f45`). Standing next to an inlet, its lines pass round the camera and read as scattered dashes — a step into the room shows the flow. **Left:** wind through doors into the next room; chips only for the room you stand in.

**LITE DONE (2026-10-18, branch `s27/lite` off `s27/finish` 9640d9b, one Opus agent, not merged; commits f3e27b4 · be0162e · 4baef87 · 02369c6 + the numbers commit; typecheck green; `npx vitest run src/three src/viewer src/furnish` 330 passed + 1 todo; measured in Firefox, numbers below).** Extra item 1, his words 2026-10-18 "prepare Plotline for devices without a GPU or much RAM … i want things not to fail at all", and the same day "give them a choice". **What he sees:** bottom left of every 3D view (buyer links, Preview 3D, the built-in flats) a **Quality** menu Lite / Low / High; the one the device check recommends is labelled "(recommended for this device)"; the first time 3D opens in a browser the box is open with a one-line reason and OK (MANUAL §2 "Choosing quality"). His pick is kept in that browser (`plotline.quality`) and never overridden; `?quality=lite|low|high` in a link forces it. **The device check (`src/viewer/quality.ts recommend`, run once per page by `probeDevice`: a throw-away WebGL 2 context asking for the fast chip):** Lite when the renderer string names software drawing (SwiftShader, llvmpipe, softpipe, "Microsoft Basic Render Driver" / WARP) or built-in graphics (Intel HD / UHD / Iris — not Arc —, Mali, Adreno, PowerVR, VideoCore, Apple GPU on a phone), `navigator.deviceMemory` ≤ 4 (Chrome only), `hardwareConcurrency` ≤ 4, or no WebGL 2; else High. **While walking** (PlotlineScene `sample`): 2 s after the first movement, the median of 45 frame times taken while the camera moves; worse than 30 fps (> 35 ms, rAF steps by 16.7) → if nobody picked: one tier down, remembered as this browser's recommendation (`plotline.quality.slow`, reason "slow frames"), the page opens again saying "Switched to Low: frames were slow on this device" (at most High → Low → Lite over two page loads); already Lite: its sun shadows go off ("Shadows off: frames were still slow"); a pick or a link: nothing. **What Lite does:** pixel ratio 1 (High / Low: up to 2), no MSAA (`antialias: false`; a tier change opens the page again, since antialias is fixed at context creation — the finishes chosen ride along as `?c=`), no GTAO pass (as Low), 1024 shadow map (as Low), anisotropy 1 (`capabilities.getMaxAnisotropy` → 1, three clamps every texture), pictures at half size (`capabilities.maxTextureSize = 512`: three's own upload path halves every 1k picture, 2k to a quarter — `ponytail:` the decoded originals stay in the browser's memory; glTF pictures are also asked half size at decode via createImageBitmap `resizeWidth/Height` in a GLTFLoader plugin, `furniture.ts`), no scanned plant models (`procedural.ts lite.on`: kit `potted_plant_01/02/04` (2–6 MB each) left out as empty pieces that stay in the layout; the code-built pots keep pot and soil without their scan (pachira / calathea / anthurium / fern, 1.2–5 MB files); planter beds keep their earth without blades; trees and shrubs stay — code-built, their 1k pictures halved), and **no idle frames**: a frame is drawn only when the camera moved (pose key rounded to 0.1 mm), within 1 s of any input on the page (pointer, wheel, key), a load (`THREE.DefaultLoadingManager.onProgress`: every texture / model / sky), a piece mounted or rebuilt, a resize, or a change call (`setUnit`, finishes, hour, mode, floor) — `render.ts needsFrame`; VR always draws. All tiers now ask `powerPreference: 'high-performance'` (a two-chip laptop draws on its graphics card — likely his GTA laptop's case). **Nothing may fail:** no WebGL 2 or the renderer cannot start → "This browser or machine cannot draw 3D. Try another browser, or open on a PC." with the flat's plan (the minimap's drawing, room names) instead of a black page; the graphics lost (`webglcontextlost`) or out of memory (`gl.getError() === OUT_OF_MEMORY`, checked once a second — texture uploads that found no room) in High / Low → Lite once for that tab (`sessionStorage plotline.quality.fallback`, even over a pick), the page opens again saying "Switched to Lite: the 3D stopped on this device" / "… the graphics ran out of memory …"; already Lite → three waits for the browser to give the context back (the existing restore path reloads the sky); not back in 5 s → the same message and plan, never a frozen black canvas. **Tests** (`src/viewer/quality.test.ts`, 30, each fails without its rule): the recommendation per input, who sets the tier (fallback > link > pick > device), `needsFrame`, `slowFrames`, a Lite potted plant built empty. `engine.test.ts`'s GLTFLoader stand-in gained `register()`. **The numbers (2026-10-18, headless Firefox on this PC, one browser, 1280×720 at 1.5× device pixels like a laptop at 125–150 %, harness `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=E:/dev/tools/ms-playwright node E:/dev/tmp/s27/lite/measure.mjs` — own Vite on 5621, a fresh Firefox per run; `WARP=1` = software drawing; the test lanes were loading the machine at the same time).** Two different machines in effect — say which: **(a) the REAL graphics card** (headless Firefox drew on this PC's GTX 980, not software GL as hoped) and **(b) the SOFTWARE stand-in** for a weak laptop (Firefox `webgl.angle.force-warp`: "Microsoft Basic Render Driver", the CPU draws every pixel — the device check recommended Lite there by itself, "no graphics chip in use"). High = what every link opened in before today.

| run | tier | first frame / Ready | standing still (frames drawn per second) | turning 5 s: fps · median · p95 frame | pictures on the GPU | Firefox memory (working set · private) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) GTX 980, Type A | High | 4.5 s / 12.5 s | 50 | 52 fps · 16 ms · 29 ms | 679 MB (131 textures, 1920×1080, MSAA) | 1292 · 2393 MB |
| (a) GTX 980, Type A | Lite | 3.0 s / 14.0 s | **0** | 68 fps · 13 ms · 22 ms | **131 MB** (101 textures, 1280×720) | **771 · 1143 MB** |
| (a) GTX 980, his Banani draft | High | 3.9 s / 8.9 s | 50 | 58 fps · 14 ms · 28 ms | 551 MB | 1075 · 2005 MB |
| (a) GTX 980, his Banani draft | Lite | 3.4 s / 8.1 s | **0** | 75 fps · 13 ms · 16 ms | **115 MB** | **741 · 1096 MB** |
| (b) software, Type A | High | 3.7 s / 22.7 s | 1.5 (each 0.55 s, for nothing) | **1.8 fps · 591 ms** · 666 ms — "not usable at all" | 679 MB | 2820 · 2483 MB |
| (b) software, Type A | Lite | 2.8 s / 15.9 s | **0** | **9.4 fps · 54 ms** · 226 ms (slow-frames rule turned its sun shadows off mid-turn) | 131 MB | 1329 · 1403 MB |

"Pictures on the GPU" = every texture the scene uses at the size it is uploaded, with mipmaps (High: 1k / 2k maps, the 1k glTF maps; Lite: all at 512). On the software stand-in Lite is about 10× faster per frame and still under 30 fps — a real Intel UHD laptop is far faster than WARP; Ready is 7 s sooner there. On the real card Lite's Ready for Type A was no faster (14.0 vs 12.5 s; 14.8 vs 10.3 in the first run; the draft 8.1 vs 8.9) — not dug into. **Shots** (same view, same hour): `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s27/lite/type-a-high-vs-lite.png`, `draft-high-vs-lite.png` (real card), `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s27/lite/warp/type-a-high-vs-lite.png` (software); the singles beside them; logs `run1-with-checks.log`, `run2-gpu.log`, `warp/run-warp.log`. **What Lite looks like there:** the same rooms, furniture, finishes and light; no potted plants; jagged edges (no MSAA); without the corner shading the curtains' pleats read flat and corners lighter (as Low); glass panes read hazier (the pane blends after tone mapping without the composer's half-float buffer — Low has the same). **The checks (first run, real card, no `?quality=`):** first visit: the box open, "High (recommended for this device) · This device can show the full look …" (`check-1-first-visit.png`); a pick kept (`plotline.quality = high`); out of memory in High (getError made to say so once) → the page opened again in Lite saying "Switched to Lite: the graphics ran out of memory on this device" (`check-2a-…`); picked High again → High; the context lost in High → Lite, "Switched to Lite: the 3D stopped on this device" (`check-2b-…`); lost in Lite and not given back → after 5 s "This browser or machine cannot draw 3D. Try another browser, or open on a PC." with Type A's plan and room names (`check-3-…`); WebGL switched off in Firefox → the same message and plan at once (`check-4-no-webgl.png`). Full `npm test` once: 1189 passed, 9 skipped, 1 todo, 1 failed = `src/trace/walls.test.ts`'s 3-second time check (3056 ms on unit_sheltech_a while the test lanes loaded the machine; passes alone, 9 of 9; src/trace untouched here). **Left:** his laptop's own GPU name and a run on it (the menu's hover shows it); the decoded originals in memory (real half-size files on disk if Lite still runs out); trees and shrubs keep their full geometry in Lite; Low still draws MSAA at up to 2× pixels; Lite's hazier glass (a half-float buffer without AO would fix it at one more full-screen pass); Lite on the real card is not faster to Ready.

**Rules (his, unchanged):** branch off main, never commit to main, never deploy (he pushes main; Vercel builds from it). E: only, never C:. Firefox only (`E:/dev/tools/ms-playwright`), ≤ 3 browsers, 10-minute cap. Never hide a row; a row goes only when its problem is fixed. Every gain must show on his own path, not a script. No vision model / paid API. One Opus agent per job on a worktree (`E:/dev/Plotline/.claude/worktrees/<name>`, `node_modules` junction by `cmd //c "mklink /J …"`). Usage limit: resume agents, never restart. Plain words to him. Stop and report if a job stalls half a day with no gain. Worktrees `s26-*` can be removed (unlink junctions first).

**LOCAL AI (extra item 4, founder 2026-10-18: "my pc currently has a 8 gb vram rtx 3050 … better than nothing"; branch `s27/vision` off `s27/finish` 9640d9b, not merged; typecheck + the whole suite green: 1165 passed, 9 skipped)** It costs nothing: Ollama is an open-source program (MIT, the GitHub release zip, nothing installed, nothing on C:), Qwen2.5-VL an open-licence model (Apache-2.0), both on his own RTX 3050; no account, no key, no cloud — the server is started with `OLLAMA_NO_CLOUD=1`, the Studio refuses a server that is not this PC / its network (unless "Use it anyway" is ticked) and any "-cloud" model. **Where:** `E:/dev/tools/ollama/` = ollama.exe 0.40.6 (zip checksum verified against the release's sha256sum.txt) + `start-ollama.cmd` (sets OLLAMA_MODELS=E:/dev/tools/ollama/models, OLLAMA_ORIGINS=*, OLLAMA_NO_CLOUD=1, USERPROFILE=E:/dev/tools/ollama/home so its key / cache stay on E:, then `ollama serve` on 127.0.0.1:11434) + `models/` (qwen2.5vl:7b, Q4_K_M, 6.0 GB). **Measured:** all 29/29 layers on the GPU (CUDA, compute 8.6), 7.3 GB of 8 GB VRAM in use with the desktop (model 4.2 GB + vision 0.6 GB + KV 0.2 GB), first answer 68 s (28 s load), then 0.7–2.5 s per answer (prompt ~1200 tokens, the picture included). **Code:** `src/studio/localAI.ts` — `askLocalAI({image, question, choices})` (the one call; POST /api/chat, stream false, `images`, temperature 0, Ollama structured output {why, answer ∈ ids + not-sure}; an id not on the list = not sure; a failed call tells apart not running / blocked by CORS (an opaque no-cors probe) / model not pulled), `testLocalAI` (Settings → Test: /api/tags), `askRow` (a row's pick: logged by `review.ts logAnswer` with `by: 'ai'`, model, why — his clicks carry no tag; a fix handed back for `applyAnswer`, one undo step; Looks right / Show me shown, never applied — the AI never takes a row off the list; a fix that takes an opening out (`removesOpening`) is never applied, only suggested with an Apply button; the plan changed meanwhile = dropped), `rowPicture` / `pictureFrame` (the row's box + 1 m, red ring on its spot; `wide` = the whole wall + 1 m at up to 2×, ≤ 1024 px — not measured yet, off), `rowQuestion` / `rowChoices` (plan symbols named: a quarter-circle arc is a door …), `UNTRUSTED` (row kinds Ask AI for all skips, saying so on the row). StudioApp: one request at a time (a promise chain; the card holds one model), waits for a row's answers after each edit, Undo while the AI's fix is the last undo step, the note drops when the fix is undone. Panel: Ask AI on every Check-these row (spinner), Ask AI for all beside the heading, "Local AI (this PC, free)" under the list (server, model, Test). Tests `localAI.test.ts` (stubbed fetch: parse, apply via applyAnswer, append-only log tagged by the AI, not sure, plan changed, Test's four answers, local-only + no cloud, an opening never removed by the AI, the wide frame). **Model choice (measured, `E:/dev/tmp/s27/vision/compare.sh`, the same 13 bti Amabilia rows judged by eye — labels `labels-amabilia.json`, crops `cmp-*/row-NN.png`):** qwen2.5vl:7b with the row's box + 1 m: 5 right / 8 wrong, 2–3 s an answer; the same with the wide picture (whole wall + 1 m, 2×, ≤ 1024 px — door swings plainly visible): 3 / 10 — worse, so `wide` stays off; qwen3-vl:8b (Apache-2.0, pulled to E:, 6.1 GB): 35 of 37 layers fit on the card, 12–100 s an answer (67 s mean), 1 right / 4 wrong / 2 not sure on the first 7 labelled rows before it was stopped — slower and not better, kept on disk, not used (`think: false` is sent so a thinking model answers short). **Kept: qwen2.5vl:7b, the plain picture.** **Proof as the founder (Firefox headless, one browser, `E:/dev/tmp/s27/vision/proof.mjs`, 113 s, console clean; shots + every picture sent `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s27/vision/assure-phera-2nd6th/` — 01-traced, 02-local-ai-test (+ 02b close-up "found qwen2.5vl:7b"), 03-ask-1…5, 04-ask-all-running, 05-ask-all-done, 06-export-answers, ask-01…24.png, calls.json, plotline-answers.json):** Assure Phera 2nd–6th, S on M.BED 14'-6", Auto-trace Whole floor: 133 walls, 16 rooms, 53 rows; Local AI → Test: "found qwen2.5vl:7b"; Ask AI on 5 rows, then Ask AI for all (19 more answers, the untrusted kinds of that build skipped and saying so; rows 52 → 52); Export answers: 13 entries, all `by: "ai"` with model and why. **Night run (2026-10-18, node only, nothing in the Studio changed):** the two ideas measured on 51 rows with a choice, judged by eye, on four sheets of three styles — bti Amabilia Type A (13), Assure Phera 1st (12), Finlay Chayaneer Type B (16), Eastern Housing Sapphire Unit A (10). Rows made in node exactly as the Studio makes them (`E:/dev/tmp/s27/vision/gen.mjs`: the scorer's fixture + text cache, prepare → Whole floor, the Studio's import, marks → fixes → rowAnswers); asked through the real askLocalAI (`tune2.mjs`); (a) = the program's walls (blue), low walls / zone lines (green) and openings (magenta) drawn thin over the crop, with a sentence saying so (`crop2.ps1`); (b) = one yes / no question per kind, a small tree ending in an answer id (`yesno.mjs`: "Is a door drawn at the gap — a quarter-circle arc?", "Does the wall really stop there?", "Is the small space part of the room X next to it?" …). Labels `labels2-*.json`, scorer `score2.mjs`, crops `lab-*/row-NN.png`, answers `v-{mc,mcov,yn,ynov}-<sheet>/answers.json`. Right of the rows judged:

| kind (rows) | multiple choice | + lines drawn | yes / no | yes / no + lines |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| open room (6) | 3 | 3 | 4 | 2 |
| unnamed space (7) | 2 | 2 | 3 | 2 |
| window "faint evidence" (7) | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 |
| slider (7) | 0 | 0 | 3 | 7 |
| passage (13) | 1 | 7 | 5 | 5 |
| wall end (2) | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| drew a door in a gap (5) | 2 | 2 | 3 | 3 |

**Nothing lifted, honestly:** both ideas help on the total (12 → 18 / 24 of 51), but per kind the model mostly gives ONE answer whatever the picture: every "Window … faint evidence" got "no window" (right on the 3 faint ones, wrong on the 4 real windows), every passage the same answer in each variant (all "make it a door" with the lines drawn — right on the 7 with a swing, wrong on the 5 open ones; all "open" with yes / no), every slider "yes" with yes / no + lines (7 of 7, but all 7 were real sliders, so a constant answer scores the same). The only kind over 70 % (window / slider together with yes / no + lines, 10 of 14 = 71 %) is that constant "slider yes / window no" — not reading the picture — so no kind was moved out of `UNTRUSTED` and no code was changed. qwen2.5vl:7b (Q4, 8 GB card) does not tell a door swing from an open gap on these crops. What might: a bigger vision model (not on this card at speed), or asking about a tight crop of just the gap — tried too (`TIGHT=1`: the gap + 1 m at up to 4×, yes / no): passages 6 of 13 (12 times "open"; on Amabilia's 3'-9" passage the swing is plainly in the picture and it said "no arc"), windows 2 of 7, sliders 5 of 7 — no better. Next idea if anyone tries again: no red ring over the gap (it covers the faint swing), or a different model family.

**Rules (his, unchanged):** branch off main, never commit to main, never deploy (he pushes main; Vercel builds from it). E: only, never C:. Firefox only (`E:/dev/tools/ms-playwright`), ≤ 3 browsers, 10-minute cap. Never hide a row; a row goes only when its problem is fixed. Every gain must show on his own path, not a script. No paid API or cloud model; a free local vision model on his PC only (LOCAL AI above, 2026-10-18). One Opus agent per job on a worktree (`E:/dev/Plotline/.claude/worktrees/<name>`, `node_modules` junction by `cmd //c "mklink /J …"`). Usage limit: resume agents, never restart. Plain words to him. Stop and report if a job stalls half a day with no gain. Worktrees `s26-*` can be removed (unlink junctions first).
## ▶ START HERE — SESSION 26: AUTO-TRACE FROM 17 % TO 80 % (founder, 2026-10-15, after seeing session 25's result: "17 % is disappointing as hell … a liability, not a product"; ~3 days; run under the goal skill, checkpoints 40 → 60 → 80). Start from branch `s25/100-sheets` (merge it to main first or branch from it). No vision model yet (budget; "we will integrate AI later" — the "AI helper (optional)" slot in the panel is where it plugs in, see the end).

**Where it stands (2026-10-16, branch `s26/autotrace` at 842d492 + this commit — not merged to main, not deployed):** on the 30 scorer sheets, **421 of 522 printed rooms closed and named right = 80.7 %** (started at 204 = 39.1 % in step 0; 40 / 60 / 80 % checkpoints all met with Firefox shots under `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s26/`, typecheck + 1147 tests green); names read 455 of 522; open 70, merged 16, wrong 11, false rooms 98; slowest sheet under 60 s (`npm run score30`, 7.4 min). **On all 110 sheets, as the founder in Firefox (step 7): 1410 of 2657 printed rooms closed and named right = 53 %, against session 25's 642 = 24 % on the same sheets counted the same way (its headline "about 600 of 3600 = 17 %" used a looser denominator).** Steps 0–7 below say what each did and what is still wrong; the biggest losses left are the open living / dining areas with no size, small verandas behind thin rails, furniture lines cutting rooms, names on the wrong room, and flat finding on multi-flat sheets.

**The number.** Session 25 scored 110 real brochure sheets as the founder in Firefox: about 600 of 3600 printed rooms came out closed AND correctly named = 17 % (`Docs/AUTOTRACE_100.md` §2). The old three benchmark sheets sit at 70–95 %. The goal: 80 % on real sheets, measured the same way.

**What he saw in the shots (2026-10-15):** chairs around the dining table traced as PILLARS (Assure Hermitage, Finlay Tower 4 Type A); a basin traced as an empty "Space 5 / 6"; a shower hatch on a dark sheet (CPDL La Reina Type A) traced as a WALL through Bath-1. All one cause: the tracer takes every thick dark blob as wall or pillar and every small closed outline as a room — nothing in it knows what furniture looks like.

**STEP 0 — a scorer that runs in minutes (first half-day, nothing else before it).** The 17 % came from agents eyeballing screenshots, hours per pass; a goal loop cannot measure that way. Build: (a) pick 30 sheets from `Demo drawings/` across the drawing styles (bti grey-outline, Rangs, Sheltech CAD, Assure hollow walls, CPDL dark, Finlay, Ena / small devs scans) — `Docs/AUTOTRACE_100.md` §2 says which exist and what each one does today; (b) for each, write down ONCE the printed rooms (name + printed size + a point inside the room, image px) in a JSON next to the sheet (`expected.json` in the project folder); (c) a node scorer in `src/trace/eval/` that runs the tracer on the sheet file with that scale and counts rooms closed + named at the expected point, printing one table (sheet, printed, closed+named, closed unnamed, open, false rooms, pillars-in-rooms); (d) the Firefox harness `E:/dev/tmp/s25/run.mjs` stays the PROOF, run once per checkpoint on the 30 and once at the end on all 110. Scale per sheet = the `scale` entry already in the batch JSONs (`E:/dev/tmp/s25/batch{A..I}.json`). The node score and the Firefox score must agree on 3 sheets before the loop trusts the node score.

**STEP 0 DONE (2026-10-15, branch `s26/scorer`):** `npm run score30` (Docs/AUTOTRACE_SCORER.md) traces the 30 sheets of `src/trace/eval/sheets30.json` (604 printed rooms, every drawing style) exactly as the Studio does and prints one table — **starting number: 204 of 522 printed flat rooms closed and named right = 39.1 %** (with the core 214 of 604; names read 292 of 522 = 56 %); open 265, false rooms 82 (30 of them the tracer's own AOD guesses), pillars in rooms 67, wrong names 19. ~6 min cached (text cache `E:/dev/tmp/s26/text`), ~9.5 min fresh. Firefox agrees: on bti Cosmopolis T4, Rangs Torukabbo A and CPDL Skymark D the Studio's exported unit is identical to node's (walls, corners, names, pillars) and scores the same (8/9, 8/13, 9/15) — shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s26/step0`, harness `E:/dev/tmp/s26/run.mjs` (= s25's + Export). The 39 % is these 30 sheets, not comparable 1:1 with the 110-sheet 17 %.

**STEP 1 — furniture is not building (biggest, ~45 sheets of wrong names / false rooms / pillars, plus the three shots above).** Rules, no recognition: (1) measure the sheet's wall thickness once (wall tracks already do) — any line thinner than that is never a wall; (2) a closed outline smaller than about 2 m × 2 m that does not touch a wall line is furniture (bed, sofa, table + chairs, toilet, basin, wardrobe) — paint it out before walls and before rooms, as `paperOver` does for size tables; (3) a pillar is only a solid square ON a wall line — a solid blob in the middle of a room is a chair; (4) a dark sheet's hatch (shower, tiles) is thin lines close together — texture, never a wall (`raster.ts normalPolarity` already inverts dark pages; the hatch filter goes next to it). Where: `src/trace/raster.ts` (before `outlinedWalls`), `src/trace/solve.ts prepare`. Score after: false rooms and pillars-in-rooms near zero on the 30; no named room lost.

**STEP 1 DONE (2026-10-15, branch `s26/furniture` off `s26/autotrace`, not merged; 1089 tests + typecheck green).** Four rules, no recognition: (1) a dark block is a COLUMN only when a straight line no thicker than a wall runs out of one of its sides for 0.4 m (`tracks.ts wallLeaves`, `PILLAR.leaveM`) — a chair, sofa or bedside table in the floor is no column; (2) a closed piece of ink touching no wall, ≤ 3 m, wider than a wall and holding no wall band (bed, sofa, dining set, chair, WC, the "TYPE-C ±1362 SFT" box) is left out of the wall tracing and of the thin-line faces (`raster.ts furnitureMask`, whitened through the plant mask); (3) hatch — ≥ 4 thin lines crossing 40 cm — likewise (`raster.ts hatchMask`); (4) an unlabelled thin-line strip ≤ 0.75 m deep and twice as long (wardrobe, counter, vanity, shoe cabinet) is no AOD (`faces.ts FACES.cupboardM`). The sheet itself is NOT painted (painting it moved the fitted rooms and the sheet's ink grey and churned whole sheets: −3 rooms); the ink grey stays the one read with the furniture in. **Score (`npm run score30`): 204 → 205 of 522 closed and named right (39.3 %), false rooms 82 → 65, pillars in rooms 67 → 68, wrong names 19 → 20, merged 21 → 20, names read 292 → 296.** The pillar column is the proxy's: La Reina Amber's pick grew (0 → 14 there: furniture beside long wooden furniture lines on a 29 px/m page); on the other 29 sheets 67 → 54, and about 40 of those 54 are real columns of rooms still open (Ixora 6, Phera ~7, Peak 4, Sapphire 4, Fernhill 3 …). Rooms lost, each looked at: Phera TOILET (its "right" was a 12 m² room holding Dining + Toilet, closed by walls hanging on the dining chairs), Pearl VER (a plank line of the wood floor had happened to close it at its printed size), Peak KITCHEN and one Silvi room (whole-sheet churn from a changed wall list); gained Magnifico +2, Ixora +2, Fernhill +1. Firefox (`run.mjs`, as the founder) agrees with node on Hermitage, Ixora E+F, Water Lily — same states room by room; shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s26/step1` (Hermitage: no column in the floor, the dining table and its chairs and the sofas clean — three short wall pieces remain where a sofa end or a plant pot touches the glass line). **Still wrong:** the 65 false rooms left are mostly real unprinted spaces (the lobby before a door, AC ledges, veranda-end boxes, stair landings, voids) and pieces of split rooms — not furniture; furniture drawn AGAINST a wall still reads as building (a bed head on its wall, kitchen counters, La Reina's wooden TV units and plant pots); a wardrobe whose lines are wall-thick on a low-res sheet (Magnifico "Space 3") is still a room; the founder's La Reina "Type A" shower-hatch shot is not on one of the 30 sheets (not measured). **For step 2:** 263 printed rooms are open; 154 of them on 13 sheets where the walls themselves are hardly read (hollow / grey / thin-line / 29 px/m: Gladiolus, Palm Spring, Azmary, Jahan Lodge, Aftabnagar, Mir, Magnifico, Hermitage, La Reina, Amabilia, Peak, La Montana A/B) — a printed size cannot close a room with no walls; 109 on the 17 sheets that work: verandas / balconies 39 (railing or glass side), living / dining / foyer 24 (open plan), toilets 17 and bedrooms 16 (a door gap with no swing read, a glass front), kitchens 6, other 7.

**STEP 2 — close open rooms with the printed size (~95 sheets, the biggest lost-room bucket; `AUTOTRACE_STRATEGY.md` §6 Level 1).** Every room prints its size ("M.BED 11'-0" x 14'-6""). A room with three walls and one open side: the printed size says where the fourth side is — draw a guessed glass wall (two / three parallel thin lines there), a low wall (railing / planter edge, printed VER / BALCONY), or a zone line (open plan: DINING next to LIVING), and keep it only if the closed room matches the print within tolerance, no named room loses its name, no new loose end (his §5 decision of 2026-10-09: "only if you can make it strong enough"). Each drawn guess gets its Check-these row ("Drew a glass wall to close Dining — right?"). Where: `src/trace/solve.ts` after rooms derive, using the label's parsed size (`text.ts parseSize`) and `core` to add the wall. Score after: closed+named on the 30 ≥ 60 %.

**STEP 2 DONE (2026-10-15, branch `s26/close` off `s26/autotrace`, not merged; 1101 tests + typecheck green; commits bf171b5 … the HANDOFF commit).** `src/trace/close.ts closeRooms`, run on the whole sheet's draft in `solve.ts prepareTraces` (so the flat pick, the face re-read and the names see it): a printed name standing in no closed room (or sharing one with another printed name) gets the fewest guessed pieces that close it — a **door** where a wall line stops 0.55–1.8 m short of the wall ahead with nothing drawn (passage over 1.1 m; never a solid wall), a **glass wall** on two / three thin lines, a **low wall** on one thin line of a VER / balcony / terrace, a **zone line** only for open-plan kinds (living, dining, foyer, open kitchen) and only with a printed size read, a wall under 0.5 m for a corner the trace left open — kept only when the name's room closes alone, within 0.3 m of its print (none read: drawn evidence only, ≤ 3 pieces, a size its kind can have), no room already closed around one name changes, no small nameless space (< 4 m²) is cut off, nothing starts or ends on furniture (a joined wall run under 6 m), no line runs along a wall or past the room, a tiled floor's grid is no glass. One Check-these row per closed room says what was drawn and asks ("Drew a 2'-10" door in the gap (no door swing is drawn there) to close Toilet — no printed size was read to check it. A door, or open?"); its buttons (`studio/issues.ts guessFixes`, also on the trace's older one-piece rows) answer it in one click: Make it a wall / low wall / zone line, Make it open / a door, Take it out. **Why door gaps stayed open (the brief's question):** `roomsOnTracks` decides a gap only along ONE track line and only where a fitted room edge reads it — a room whose size was not read has no fit, so nothing reads its gap; White Lake / Water Lily / Hermitage draw doors as a leaf line or a light arc, the wall stops at the jamb and the far jamb is a PERPENDICULAR wall (no track gap at all); `buildGraph carryEnds` carries a free end only where the same ink runs across (the old "a gap with nothing drawn is never bridged"). **Score (`npm run score30`): 205 → 254 of 522 closed and named right (39.3 % → 48.7 %; the 40 % checkpoint crossed at bf171b5 with 255), open 263 → 213, merged 20 → 15, false rooms 65 → 66, wrong names 20 → 22, core 11 → 17 of 82; Check these rows over the 30 sheets 1467 → ~1480 (each room closed with an unread size gains its "type the size" row).** 56 rooms closed on the 30 sheets: with a door 31, a glass wall 22, a zone line 6, a carried wall 10, a low wall 1 (railings were already merge.ts's); 11 match their print, 6 within 0.3 m, 39 had no size read. Lost 1 right room: Sapphire STLT, named "Toilet 5" by a fill-colour guess, became "Space 9" when Master Bed closed beside it (its name was never read). Old benchmarks (solve eval, rooms matched of 116): OCR 48 → 53, new reader 74 → 76, oracle 81 → 83. **Firefox (`E:/dev/tmp/s26/step2/run2.mjs` = run.mjs + click the first "Drew …" row and its fix by mouse, then Ctrl+Z), shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s26/step2`:** White Lake 3rd floor whole floor — "Type A: all 12 room names read on it are closed and named", Type B the same (24 of 24; was 12); clicked "Make it open (no door)" on the Toilet row — toast "Ctrl+Z undoes it", the row left the list (`…-typeA-row.png`, `…-row-fixed.png`); Chayaneer Type B 13 of 16 (was 9): the Dining Space zone line row, "Make it a wall" clicked; Ixora E+F 16 of 22 (was 8). Browser = node room by room on all three (`SCORE30_BROWSER`). Note: White Lake scores 20 of 24 at the scorer's exact scale and 24 at the mouse's (0.2 % apart) — the size gate sits near its edge on a few rooms. **Still open:** of the 213, 148 are on the 13 sheets whose walls are hardly read; on the 17 that work, 34 printed rooms whose name was never read (no closing can name them — step 3) and 35 whose name was read but mostly not their size, so no zone line may close them (Ixora / Siddiques / Water Lily living-dining, Torukabbo LIVING+DINING, Skymark Dining & Family Living); the rest are bedrooms whose door gap is a fragment soup (Hermitage Child Bed) or whose print does not match the drawn room (Water Lily BED-3: drawn 12'9" × 13'7", printed 12' × 11'). **What the 13 wall-less sheets need (for after step 3):** Gladiolus — hollow walls (light body, thin dark outline) + light-grey sans names (0 read); Palm Spring — names rotated 90° + walls in slivers; Azmary — grey-band walls on a mid-grey page with a white outline + small light-grey names (0 read); Jahan Lodge — names only in a numbered legend (01–09) + grey walls on a striped dark background; Aftabnagar — CAD thin double-line walls + names printed sideways + a watermark; Mir MR Memento — light hollow walls on a grey page under a full tile grid (big page, 2 names read); Magnifico / Amabilia — bti's grey-filled walls between thin outlines (outlinedWalls reads part); Hermitage — light hollow walls (windows read, walls partly); La Reina — tiny print (~5 px letters, 29 px/m) + brown wood-tone walls in pieces; The Peak — labels rotated 90° (duplex) and columns in rooms; La Montana A / B — white lines on a dark page (inverted), the key plan taken as the flat, some labels rotated.

**STEP 3 — read the text at full size (~12 sheets "print is small", ~40 half-read names, ~18 rotated).** Big pages (2400–6000 px) are shrunk before reading, so small print dies: read in strips at full resolution; try each strip rotated 90° for sideways labels; the 45-px title figures ("2279 SFT") fail the reader's height filter (`cleanForOcr`) — widen it. Where: `src/trace/text.ts readText`. Score after: names read on the 30 ≥ 90 % of printed.

**STEP 3 DONE (2026-10-15, branch `s26/text` rebased on `s26/autotrace` 77e15c9 = after step 2, not merged; typecheck + tests green).** The reader (`text.ts readText`) now reads the page at its own size in passes and keeps the best reading of each spot: (1) the page as before; (2) one strict pass per height the page prints text at (`textHeights`: letters standing in a word vote for their height — names, sizes, title figures up to 170 px), at twice the contrast, keeping the pieces a floor-tile line or a fill's speckle cut off a name (`cleanForOcr` opts `charH` / `orphans`), read raw and from its mask; (3) white print on dark ground inverted (`darkGround`); (4) the page turned a quarter each way (`rotateCW` / `rotateCCW`, boxes turned back with `boxBack`), at the heights where the turned page shows words (votes ≥ a fifth of the page's own, ≥ 0.75 × its glyph height) — then a nameless size gets the strip above it re-read, a wider crop catches letters glued to a basin or a WC, a size table's whole-row line beats its pieces, one label read twice by two passes is kept once, a title's "SIZE" + "2152" read apart become the flat area. The gate (`nameOk` / `roomName`): a read names a room only in the sheet's room words (one letter off at most) with one number — a size's digits glued on, two labels in one ("BED 1 VER 1 TD 1" → three labels), a stray line stacked on a name never name a room or change its kind; numbers kept ("BED D4" → BED 04, "BED-04/HOME OFFICE" keeps its 04 — `normaliseName` reads each side of a "/" apart, which the scorer's `nameRight` shares); a height pass's size is used only when tesseract confirms it on the page itself (its mask read Amabilia's 13'-6" as 13'-0"), else it is a "type this size" row. Legends: "[06 CHILD BED" numbers, a numbered row with no size still makes the column, a legend in a dark box is a table. OCR workers: half the cores, 3–6 (`readInFaces` in parallel too). **Score (`npm run score30` on the merged tree, against step 2's 254): names read 298 → 434 of 522 (57 → 83 %; target 470 not reached), closed + named right 254 → 351 (48.7 → 67.2 % — the 60 % checkpoint is crossed), wrong names 22 → 16, unnamed 18 → 3, open 213 → 137, core 17 → 20 of 82, false rooms 66 → 85, pillars in rooms 44 → 46.** A name read with its size is what step 2's closing needs: Gladiolus 0 → 10 rooms, La Montana A + B 10 → 28, Phera 6 → 13, Amabilia 3 → 10, Magnifico 3 → 11, Chayaneer 13 → 16 (all 16), Skymark 10 → 14. Rooms lost (5, each looked at, all right before by a guess or by luck): Phera CHILD BED (now merged with its toilet — the walls moved with the new text erase), La Montana A's LIFT (core, open now), Jahan Lodge MASTER BATH (its guessed "Toilet 1" became "Veranda 1"), Mir's two bottom VERs (guessed "Ver 4" / "Ver", open now). False rooms +19: more rooms are fitted from read sizes and pieces between them close (Amabilia 1 → 8, Magnifico 4 → 9). **Time:** the text read is 13 s a sheet on average, at most 33 s (was ~7); but on the merged tree two sheets go over the 60 s rule in node: Kanakchapa 115 s with the text read fresh (102 s with it cached) and Sapphire 66 s — measured on Kanakchapa: 50 s of it is `close.ts closeRooms` (step 2), which grows with the printed labels it now has (step 2's own baseline there was 57 s); the closing stage needs a time budget or a faster candidate search. Every other sheet is 4–45 s. **Firefox (`E:/dev/tmp/s26/run.mjs`, as the founder, shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s26/step3`) = node on all three, room by room:** Accept Aftabnagar (sideways names: 11 names read on the flat line, 3 of 12 closed — its walls are hardly traced), Eastern Housing Sapphire (big page: 13 of 15 names closed and named on the flat line, the "SIZE 2152 SFT" area read, read 71 s + traced 37 s), Ena Jahan Lodge (white legend on dark blue: Master Bed, Master Ver, Child Ver named by their numbers, 890 sft read). **Still unread (88):** names read on a sheet whose walls are hardly traced are not counted on a flat (Palm Spring reads 5 of 12 sideways names, Jahan Lodge 4 + its legend — the scorer counts names inside the traced flat only); tiny grey print on a busy floor (Magnifico 10, La Reina 8 at 6 px), names touching furniture or with a bed drawn through them (Pearl's G.BED, Mir's TOILETs on blue tiles), white names on foliage or wood (Pearl's GREEN / VER — not dark enough to count as dark ground), names no word list knows (GRAVEL, GYM, SWIMMING POOL, STAIRCASE WITH FIRE DOOR, S/A — 5).

**STEP 2b DONE (2026-10-15, branch `s26/close` reset to `s26/autotrace` fbb505d, not merged; 1117 tests + typecheck green).** The closing search was slow on dense sheets after step 3's reader (more names read = more names to close): Kanakchapa 109 s in node (closeRooms ~50 s of it), Magnifico 46 s. Cause: every candidate re-solved the rooms of a 28 m box around the name (600 of Kanakchapa's 1455 walls), every point check re-built every room outline, and the wall runs (loose ends × all walls) were re-joined for every name. Now (`close.ts`): the box is the name's reach + 1 m (`CLOSE.boxM`), the runs are worked out once per draft (again only after a piece goes in), each solve's outlines and boxes once, a second try only for names a new piece went in near, and the kept pieces go into the whole draft only when they all fit there. **Per sheet (node, text cached): Kanakchapa 109 → 43 s (closeRooms ~50 → ~3.5 s), Magnifico 46 → 22 s, every sheet ≤ 43 s; Sapphire 46 → 43 s is not the closing (< 0.5 s): ~29 s tracing its big page and ~20 s reading its faces / types.** Results identical (351, every printed room the same state). Firefox on Kanakchapa (`E:/dev/plotline-shots/s26/step2b`): "Point at a flat" after 54 s, the flat click traced in 37 s, 11 of 14 — the same rooms as node. **New, the 19 false rooms step 3 exposed:** none come from the closing (switched off they stay 8 on Amabilia, 9 on Magnifico). Taken: `close.ts joinLeftovers` — a nameless space under 4 m² split off a named room by walls with no door joins it when the room alone is off its print and the two together match it (the 2026-10-03 / 2026-10-09 rules): a shower area behind its curtain line, a veranda's planter box at its end — **352 of 522 (Nirvana VERANDAH), false rooms 85 → 81, no right room lost.** Left: Amabilia — AC outdoor boxes, K.VER and S.BED split in two by a stray line (one half holds the read name, so it is no nameless space), a lobby piece, a 5.4 m² wardrobe strip, "Bed" read on an AC box; Magnifico — beds and wardrobes traced as walls (grey-band walls, furniture against walls: step 1's leftover) and the AC / shaft stack by the lifts.

**STEP 4 DONE (2026-10-15, branch `s26/walls` rebased on `s26/autotrace` fbb505d = after step 3, not merged; typecheck + 1105 tests green; commits d73ca8a, 41463e1 + this HANDOFF commit).** Walls that are not solid dark ink, all in `raster.ts`: (1) `normalPolarity`: a plan printed as a light PANEL on a dark page (its biggest light piece fills ≥ 0.3 of its box; La Montana's white line work ≈ 0.1) is no longer turned over — the page round it (its commonest tone ± 25, reached from the border through that tone) is made paper (`paperPage`); (2) `outlinedWalls` generalised from bti's grey walls to any band between two thin lines: a line = 40 darker than beside it (a 1 px grey outline counts), any fill — grey (darker than the floor both sides) or paper (hollow walls) —, the fill ≥ 0.07 m (a window's panes are closer), no grid (three equal crossings in a row: tiles, planks, a four-line window), no hatch; with a paper fill only the sheet's commonest crossing widths count (± 1 px, ≥ 15 % of the top); corners and T's closed by the paper the bands wall in (thinner than a wall, under 2 m, touching them); kept only where they join a band ≥ 1 m long (also through a column); gate: the long outlined bands at least twice the long solid ones — it fires on Amabilia, Gladiolus, Phera, Hermitage and Aftabnagar only (solidLong 0 there) and returns null on the 25 others (the nearest, Azmary, has 0.83 outlined per solid); (3) `solve.ts prepareTraces` hands the painted bands' half width to `traceWalls` (its own guess read the thin lines: 2 px on Gladiolus). Tests: `raster.test.ts` (paper between grey lines on white, the CAD look on a grey page, an armchair frame not painted, black walls → null, a panel on a dark page). **Score (`npm run score30`, against the merged tree's 351): 351 → 382 of 522 closed and named right (67.2 → 73.2 %), open 137 → 100, names read 434 → 443, wrong names 16 → 19, merged 15 → 16, false rooms 85 → 105, pillars in rooms 46 → 34.** Per sheet (right, open): Hermitage 10 → 18 (10 → 1), Azmary 1 → 11 (13 → 2), Palm Spring 1 → 7 (11 → 2), Gladiolus 10 → 13 (5 → 1), Aftabnagar 4 → 7 (7 → 3), Phera 13 → 14; the 17 solid-wall sheets identical row by row; Amabilia, Magnifico, Peak, La Montana A/B, La Reina, Jahan, Mir unchanged. **STEP 6A DONE (2026-10-15, branch `s26/legend` off `s26/autotrace` 9813cda, not merged; typecheck + 1142 tests green; commit f576118 + this HANDOFF / MANUAL commit). Target met: +14 (≥ 14 of the 22 asked).** Rules (`text.ts` / `solve.ts` naming / `types.ts`, each tested in `text.test.ts` / `solve.test.ts`): (1) `findTables`: a legend's names column read as ONE item over several rows ("F. Living Living Dining Kitchen Bed1", La Reina's 8 px legend) is split into rows by the sizes on its right, one per baseline (`splitStack`: every row numbered or none); a row keeps its size line's unsure reading as `TableRow.sizeGuess` (`rowSize`) and `applyTables` hands it to the plan label as `sizeGuess` (5A's guess pin then checks it); a legend's 1–2 px ruled lines are no wall ink (`darkOutside` counts a pixel only when its four neighbours are dark too); `nameKey` reads "BED 01" = "Bed 1" and drops stray letters ("J BALCONY" = "BALCONY"); feet-marks-lost "11-06\" x 10-00\"" is a size (`marksLost`). (2) numbered legends: a row whose number was misread ("DIS IIVING", "[00 KITCHEN") takes its place in the column (`numberRows`); when a numbered row has no plan number, the page's two-figure marks (one blob two figures wide, or two side by side: `numberMarks`) are read once more with figures only, kept at confidence ≥ 90 (Jahan's "06" in a white disc is one blob, no text line); a plan number must be no taller than twice its row (a 71 px "1" read off the page's stripes had taken row 01). (3) a bedroom / bath / veranda name read with no number (or a lone 0) gets the name views and its mask read; a view reading the same letters with a number gives it, when no view reads another (`plusNumber`: Magnifico BED → BED-4, Amabilia BATH → BATH-3, Silvi BATH-0 → BATH-01, Fernhill VER 0 → VER 03, Siddiques TOI → TOILET-2). (4) `groupWords`: a garbled line twice the name's width is never the name's first line (Azmary's "Fr = | 1] MASTER BATH" put the label outside its room). (5) `pickTraces` naming: a label whose number was lost takes the name of the ONE size-table row of its name no label took whose size is the room's within 20 % (`tableRow`: La Reina BED → Bed 3, row "Its number was not read: named Bed 3 from the size table … — check"); a kind guessed from a fill colour likewise takes its row's name; a guessed "Toilet 1" never repeats a printed "TOILET 01" (`nameKey`). **Score (`npm run score30`, my own base run of 9813cda = 396, false 103): 396 → 410 of 522 (75.9 → 78.5 %), wrong names 17 → 10, unnamed 6 → 6, merged 16 → 17, open 87 → 79, false rooms 103 → 101, names read 444 → 455; with the core 418 → 432 of 604.** Per rule: legend split + guesses + table naming La Reina 6 → 11 (BED 04, TOILET 04, DRESS, LIVING, DINING, F. **STEP 6B DONE (2026-10-15, branch `s26/ends` rebased on `s26/autotrace` c473f15 = after 6A, not merged; typecheck + 1147 tests green; commits 90b75d9, 5436aaa, ac3964b + this HANDOFF / MANUAL commit). Target met: +12 on its own base (≥ 10 asked), +11 on the merged tree — 421 of 522 = 80.7 %, the 80 % mark (418) crossed.** Rules, all in `close.ts` (tests beside it in `close.test.ts`), plus the row anchor in `solve.ts pickTraces`: (1) **a wall end beside a line** — `rayHit(…, near)`: a wall end stopping up to 0.4 m (`CLOSE.nearM`) beside a ray stops it (a jog onto the end, `Piece.via`), only where nothing is hit straight within a metre further; on for loose-end carries, the lines between two names and the printed-size sides; a corner's ray carried so is tried LAST (`late`), only when nothing else closes the room (on all rays it crowded the 60 candidates and lost La Montana A LIVING and three Magnifico rooms — measured per call site, kept only where loss-free): La Montana B DINING, Gladiolus PARENT'S BED, Ixora both LIVING - DINING, Mir VER = 5; (2) **a hair off inside a window** — `insertPiece`: a piece whose jog point lands inside another wall's window ≤ 6 cm (`hairM`) from the end it meets runs straight onto that end (5A's "door piece refused by insertPiece": the jog vertex sat on the window wall's centre line): Hermitage TOILET (S BED un-merged), Peak LIVING+DINING and PLANTER = 3; (3) **open-plan rooms side by side** — a candidate that closes an open-plan room within its print (main rectangle) but leaves only other open-plan names in it (`evalSet(…, holding)`) gets a second line from the candidates (one crossing the first is cut there; the first may run on past the room — it is both rooms' side): Accept Aftabnagar Dining + Family Living = 2; (4) **a stray line** — `joinLeftovers`: the nameless half may be any size (was < 4 m²) when the two together match the print (the name cut off in a strip); with NO size read, a wall under 1.2 m between a bed / bath / kitchen / maid / study / closet / utility / pantry / prayer face and a nameless one goes (a tile line traced as a low wall): Sapphire BED = 1 (6A's hand-over); (5) the closing row sat at the middle of a piece's two ends — off the wall for a jog piece, so Gladiolus Parents Bed had no row: it now sits on the straight part. **Score (`npm run score30`): own base 9813cda 396 → 408 (merged 16 → 15, false rooms 103 → 101); **Rules for the loop (all binding):** one Opus agent per step on a worktree off the working branch; measure on the 30-sheet scorer after every change, never by eye; a step is done only when the Firefox harness agrees on the 30 (proof = shots, as the founder: drop the sheet, S on a printed size, Auto-trace); never hide rows or marks — a row goes only when its problem is fixed; every gain on his own path (drop sheet, click Auto-trace), no script-only path; mouse editing in the acceptance too (he caught a two-week pillar bug no run had ever clicked); HANDOFF + MANUAL in the same merge; `npm run typecheck` + `npm test` before every commit; never main until he says; never C:; Firefox only, ≤ 3 in parallel, 10-min cap per run; a usage limit kills every agent at once — resume with a message, never restart; no backticks inside double-quoted bash.

**AI later (his decision 2026-10-15: not now, broke).** After steps 1–3 the leftovers are small crops (a blob the rules could not call, a name the reader could not read), so a vision model costs cents per sheet: Claude Haiku 5.5 for crop questions ("chair, pillar or wall?"), Sonnet 5.5 for a whole unreadable sheet; it plugs into the existing "AI helper (optional)" slot (`src/trace/ai.ts`). Gemini has had a free tier — check its terms then, do not plan on it. Also his later idea: the developer's own 3D massing model (what the brochure renders are made from) imported as the building's outside shell, our traced floors inside.

**The plan, in full, with the measured state and six levels: `Docs/AUTOTRACE_STRATEGY.md` §6.** Short form:
- **Measured now (the product's own path, no AI): 71 of 116 rooms found on the five hand-traced flats** — Sheltech A 15/21, Sheltech B 14/20, BTI Type A 18/27, Type B 19/22, Type C 23/26. Perfect names would still give 71: the room BOUNDARIES are the cap (glass fronts, railings, planter edges, shaft walls, open plan are not thick walls). Rooms go missing three ways: MERGED into a neighbour (verandas, AODs, lobbies), SPLIT by a line that is no wall (counters, wardrobes, column rows, unclosed door bridges), or NO FACE (never closes).
- **Level 0 — measure in the product, every sheet** (an agent as the founder in Firefox, every flat sheet in `Demo drawings/`, rooms printed vs closed-and-named vs "Space N" vs missing, overlay shots) → `Docs/AUTOTRACE_SCOREBOARD.md` = the acceptance for all later levels; plus 3 more hand-traced flats as ground truth (Banani, DMD, a Sheltech level other than L2 if it differs — check, never assume).
- **Level 1 — close rooms on their real boundaries** (glass / railing / open edges classified and shown as glass wall / low wall / zone line, each a "check this" row). Needs the founder's §5 decision (show non-wall boundaries? recommended yes). Swallows the parked glass-front problem. Done when ≥ 95 of 116 and ≤ 1 room missing per scoreboard flat.
- **Level 2 — stop splitting rooms on non-walls** (an unnamed face bounded by thin lines / gaps joins its named neighbour; door bridges close or become a row). Done when "split" is gone from the eval.
- **Level 3 — every printed name and size lands in its room** ("Space 4" → Toilet 1, the Kitchen's size, the TYPE label kept, a typed size makes a size row).
- **Level 4 — the whole floor in one click** (all flats + core, `deriveFlats` groups, TYPE labels name, area line per flat).
- **Level 5 — the person fixes the rest in seconds** (rows say which room and offer the one-click fix; "17 of 18 printed rooms closed; missing: Veranda 1").
- **Order 0 → 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5**; one Opus agent per level on a worktree off main; Levels 1–2 plan first (core + trace); every level ends with a Firefox run on the sheets, the scoreboard re-run, HANDOFF + MANUAL in the same merge, typecheck + tests green.

**Level 3 — PARTLY DONE (2026-10-09, Opus agent; MERGED on main at 7d914d7, 1012 tests + typecheck green, pushed).** Every gain is on the founder's path (drop the sheet, S, Auto-trace): (1) **reading** — `text.ts`: every line not read as a name / size / area gets three more views (the new "own" view = the raw crop with only the line's own glyph blobs, the size line under it and the wall beside it whited out) with the capital-letter alphabet; `readInFaces`: after the walls, every closed face no name was read in is read whole (tesseract's page layout on the raw crop, one block at 40 px + sparse text at 32 px) — it finds names the line finder lost (Sheltech TOILET 1 crossed by a door arc, PDR touching a basin, TOILET 2, VER). A lift / lobby / stair name found only by a second look is never added (the west LIFT read that way made the core rules cut Type B's foyer and Bed 2 off). `trimName` drops stray letters at a name's ends ("I TOILET", "BED LQ", "HELP RO RS" → Help); VIR = VER. (4) **typed sizes** — `review.ts typedSizeRows`: a printed size typed by hand (label box, R, Name it) gets the live size row (shown only while the room differs; Looks right dismisses it). **Firefox as the founder, before (317b08e) → after, `Docs/AUTOTRACE_SCOREBOARD.md` "After Level 3":** names read / closed + named / Space N on a printed name / Check these / Issues — Sheltech L2 A 8→14 / 8→12 / 3→0 / 44→34 / 9→3; L2 B 11→12 / 10→10 / 0 / 26→25 / 2→2; L4 A 8→13 / 6→9 / 3→1 / 50→51 / 9→9; BTI Type A 21 / 19 / 0 / 46→45 / 4; Banani L2-6 A 6→9 / 1 / 0 / 37→40 (+3 "is open" rows for newly read names) / 30. Node: reader 81 → 86 of 116 names, sizes right 46 → 48 of 87, 0 misread; solver 71 → 72 of 116. **Still not read, and why:** the Kitchen's and Toilet 1's printed sizes on Sheltech L2 (their glyphs touch a counter line / a door arc and are dropped with the drawing — a cut-free-run rescue brought the Kitchen's line back but read it wrong and lost a BTI AOD: not kept), the light grey TOILET over its WC (5'-8"×4'-1", Sheltech, every level), TOILET 3 next to the kitchen (letters touch the basin and wall), SUNSHADE / PLANTER (4 px), red TYPE-B, toilet numbers in a few face reads ("Toilet" for TOILET 1 on L2 B); Banani's 6 px names (9 of 19). Dev loop for the next reader lane: `E:/dev/tmp/s24/l3/decode.mjs` saves Firefox's own decode of a sheet as PGM/PPM — the node solver on it gives the Studio's exact result (the old fixtures differ by a few walls); scratch tests `scratch-solve` / `scratch-face` in `E:/dev/tmp/s24/l3/`.

**Levels 1–2 — PARTLY DONE (2026-10-09 night, Opus agent; MERGED on main at 0e6cc85, 1018 tests + typecheck green, pushed). Open for the founder: the 6" keep / 12" drop tolerances on a guessed railing or glass edge were the agent's choice — confirm or change.** On the founder's path only (drop the sheet, S, one Auto-trace click). Plan as run: Level 1 first (close rooms on their real boundaries, under his §5 rule: strong evidence AND a passed check), then Level 2 (no split on a line that is no wall). **What Auto-trace now does:** (1) **railing** — one thin line across a track gap on the open side of a room printed VER / planter = its railing, a 1.1 m low wall (0.45 m planter kerb) on the drawn line, joined to the wall ends (`merge.ts`); a face holding a VER / veranda name closes on thin lines (faces.ts' thin-rim test waived, as for planters); (2) **glass** — two / three solid parallel pen lines ≥ 0.15 m apart = a glass front: a full-height wall that is all window, sill 0, to its top (faces.ts `glazingFrames` → `OpeningGuess.glass` → solve.ts sets sill 0, wall height — what O → Glass wall makes); two strokes closer than that = one railing line drawn double; `glassMask` takes the scale (a 5 px band of blue pen lines is glass, not a floor tint — Sheltech L4's kitchen glazing was being erased, its gap stayed undecided); (3) **the check** (solve.ts, before the labels) — each railing / glass is weighed against the printed size of the room it closes (core `printedSizeCheck`, main rectangle or outline): within 6" (`KNOBS.closeTolM`) no row; off by > 12" (`closeRejectM`) it is removed and the room stays open with its Level 5 row; in between or no size read: kept with its row; (4) **loose ends** — `solve.ts carryEnds`: a free end (or one only a low wall meets) runs on along its own line to the wall ahead (≤ 1.2 m) where the same thing is drawn across (a wall's dark band; pane lines for a window piece — a wall carried across pane lines gets a window; a thin line for a low wall), or unconditionally under 0.3 m (no opening is that narrow); a railing meets a wall drawn on its own line; the trace's "A wall ends here" row skips ends on a column (core validate's rule); (5) **Level 2** — `mergeUnread` also joins across a LOW wall (a counter / wardrobe / beam line), and a read of a drawing (1–2 strokes, WASHING MACHINE, FREEZER, FRIDGE, BEAM … SLAB) no longer counts as a space's own name (`nameLike`); (6) **core** — LIFT PIT / STRETCHER LIFT read as 'other' text are core too (`coreText`), and `dropForeignStubs` drops the core's walls left hanging off the flat. **Firefox as the founder, main 526e0e1 → branch 0b7fdc3 (`Docs/AUTOTRACE_SCOREBOARD.md` "After Levels 1–2", shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s24/l12/before-…` / `after1-…`, harness `E:/dev/tmp/s24/l12/l12.mjs` + `batch.mjs`):** count line / Check these / Issues — Sheltech L2 A 10/14 → 13/14 / 34 → 33 / 3 → 2 (+ kitchen veranda, Bed 4, Veranda 7'-5"; Bed 3 no longer tinted a flat of its own); L2 B 9/12 → 11/13 / 25 → 26 / 2 → 2 (+ Bed 4, Veranda 7'-9"); L4 A 8/13 → 9/13 / 51 → 38 / 9 → 5 (+ Kitchen); BTI img_3 Type A 19/21 → 20/21 / 45 → 42 / 4 → 3 (+ Living Room; lifts out of the flat); BTI img_2 north 16/17 → 16/17 / 49 → 46 / 2 → 0; south 14/19 → 15/19 / 53 → 46 / 11 → 7 (+ Help Bed); Banani L2-6 A 1/9 → 1/9 / 40 → 37 / 30 → 29.
