**Pillars — DONE (2026-10-09 night, Opus agent; MERGED on main at e74e30a, pushed; 1035 tests + typecheck green).** The founder's six complaints of that night, one cluster (walls meet, rooms close, columns, the R tool): (1) **a wall touching a column closes the room** — core `graph.ts joinTouching` (deriveRooms traces faces on it; the unit is never changed): a LOOSE wall end on / in a column's block (within `TOUCH_M` = ½") makes that column solid — its outline becomes edges, the walls crossing it split there (pieces keep their wall's id), the rooms go round it, the faces inside it are dropped; a loose end inside another wall's body meets that wall's centre line along its own line. Loop points that are no vertex ride on `Room.joinPts` (`roomPolygon` resolves them); `validate`: such ends are no dangling vertex. Consumers that read loop ids now go by the plan (finishes `faceWallId`, presets sides, Studio label sides, island fix, 3D daylight blockers — Preview 3D threw on a column side before that fix —, Building caps, viewer spawn). Root cause: columns were not in the wall graph and `validate` forgave an end in a column, so the room stayed open with NO issue shown. (2) **size a column from any side or corner, every column** — `StudioApp.pillarHandleAt`: eight handles on a picked column, a side moves alone (ew/ns cursor), a corner moves two, the opposite stays, snapped as a corner, whole inches; inside a block the column wins the click over the walls / openings / labels through it (a wall end right under the pointer, 4 px, still first). Root cause: only the four corner dots sized it, and vertices / labels / openings were hit-tested before the column. (3) **"the middle line doesn't align"**: no such message exists in the code (grepped snap.ts, issues.ts, review.ts, Panel, StudioApp, trace). What he saw while drawing to a column was the status line "snapped: column" (no complaint), and after it R's "Not a closed room: Wall end 9 cm short of the wall (ringed). Join walls (panel) fixes overlaps and crossings." — that message is gone (see 6). (4) **a wall on a thick wall's face or a column's face joins, no 3" stub** — a thick wall's face already joined before (seen in Firefox: Store / Study named in both runs); columns did not (= 1). `model.ahead` now also reaches a column's block (onto its face). (5) **3 cm short is joined** — `ahead` reaches a wall within its own thickness too (founder: "max(0.1 m, the other wall's thickness)"; the old reach max(0.15, 1.5 × own thickness) stays), and W commits now carry ends on like drag-end / Join walls / load do. Before: "Not a closed room: Wall end 4 cm short of the wall (ringed)…"; after: the room closes on the click, R names it. (6) **R decides on the room around the click** — `issues.leaksAround`: the loose ends whose own fix (Close the gap / Join / Extend) closes a room around the click, alone, else those all of them together need; one within reach is healed (one undo step) and the name box opens; else the message names that end, how far short and where ("the wall end 2'-3" up-left of your click (ringed) stops 0'-7" short of the wall ahead — drag its end onto that wall"), else "a wall is missing. Draw it with W." — a loose end elsewhere is never ringed. **Firefox as the founder (harness `E:/dev/tmp/s24/pil/run.mjs` + `t1`–`t5.mjs`, shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s24/pil/`, before = 526e0e1 / main 21a29e4):** columns + three walls on them (top-face middle, corner, far side) — before: R refused both rooms ("Wall end 9 / 15 cm short"), Issues showed only "No closed rooms yet"; after: Toilet and Bed named, Issues only "No entry door"; Preview 3D walks into "TOILET 3.6 m²" (it threw before the daylight fix), dollhouse clean. Sizing — before: no side handles, a side drag moved the column; after: open-floor column 1'-0" × 1'-8" → east side 1'-8" × 1'-8" → NW corner 2'-1" × 2'-1"; same on a column on a drawn 13½" wall and on a TRACED Sheltech column a traced wall runs through (2'-5" × 1'-4" → 2'-11" → 3'-5" × 1'-10"), cursor ew-resize / nwse-resize. Auto-trace main → after: Banani L2-6 A Check these 29 → 29, Issues 24 → 23, 1 of 7 names closed (same); R in its living / dining: before "Wall end 14 cm short of the wall (ringed)" (a wrong ring on the Unit A / B line), after "no wall end around this spot closes it — a wall is missing" (no ring; the trace leaves it open on several sides). Sheltech L2 A 33 / 2 / 13 of 14 → identical. **The founder's own Banani draft** (`founder-banani-unit-a-2026-10-09.json`, pinned in `src/studio/founderBanani.test.ts`): as saved 17 of 17 labels closed before and after (21 faces now: his unnamed S.Bed / S.Toilet corner closes too); WITHOUT the ten pieces he drew inside columns: before 12 of 17 (Toilet, Bed 3, Master bed, Bed 2, greens open) with NO loose end shown, after 17 of 17. Imported in Firefox: before (no pieces) R in Bed 2 refused "Wall end 10 cm short", Issues listed Bed 3 / Bed 2 / greens outside a closed room; after R closes all four rooms tried, Issues = two "Unnamed space" rows. **Left open:** R with several gaps says only "a wall is missing" (no where); the Issues row "Label is not inside a closed room" still rings the nearest open spot (openSpotsNear — leaksAround per label was too slow for every edit); a wall end on a picked column's side cannot be dragged while the column is picked; a column nothing ends on changes no room (a through-wall alone keeps the old polygon). Test edits outside this lane's code: one line in `src/trace/solve.test.ts` (graph(false) now has 1 room: core joins its corner ends). MANUAL §5 / §10 / §11.

**Level 4 — DONE for its scope, rough edges listed (2026-10-09 night, Opus agent; branch `worktree-agent-af2bd63842e3206ff`, merged with main incl. the pillar lane; 1043 tests + typecheck green; MERGED on main 88a0106, 2026-10-13, pushed).** The founder's day 9, "the whole floor in one click". **Problems found first (before = main b4c1884, Firefox as the founder):** (a) a flat's pick was chosen FROM the click — the rival rule "the print nearer the click is this flat's" and the flood's area cap ran from the click spot, so BTI 3rd floor south gave three different flats from three clicks (116 / 98 / 96 walls, 15 of 19 / 12 of 16 / 11 of 17 names) and lost its kitchen, Bath-2, study and Bed-2 (the north's BED-2 print was 476 px from the click, its own 487 px); (b) nothing stopped a flat taking the next flat's rooms: BTI 2nd floor's small west flat took Bed-3, PDR, Help Bed of the big flat through the lobby corridor and its count listed the big flat's Living Room / Dining (10 of 16), the small east one "7 of 10 — missing Living Room", BTI north took the south flat's PDR (a face beside its Bed-2 whose only door is into the south dining); (c) one traced flat came out in pieces: Sheltech L2 Type B's Bed 2 closed but untinted (its door not found), a click in that Bed 2 traced ONE room and said "Auto-trace is made for flats. On a ground floor, basement or rooftop …"; (d) that message is not a sheet-kind guess at all — it shows whenever fewer than 2 rooms are labelled (Banani L2-6: thin-line walls → 1 room → "ground floor"); (e) the red "TYPE-B (L/O)" is lost by the line finder inside its red box, so Type B could not be named; (f) BTI's lifts are read as text ("LIFT PIT", "STRETCHER LIFT"), not room names, so their faces came out "Space N". **What changed (code):** `solve.ts` — the pick is split into `selectFlat` (one flat from a spot, never a room another flat holds, nor its floor) and `floorParts` (the floor's flats chosen ONCE per sheet, cached: seeds = the printed living / dining / kitchen names, biggest first; each seed no flat holds starts the next flat with the earlier flats' rooms as rivals and their floor as a wall; a flat must hold a bedroom's name + one more; a printed room no flat reached joins the flat it shares most walls with); a click = the flat it lies in (or whose rooms its face touches), else the old pick; `pickFloor` / `pickTraces(p, undefined, true)` = all flats + the lobby / lift / stair faces between them; every traced room's label is pinned (`RoomLabel.flat`) to its flat's name — "Type X" from the stamp's TYPE, else "Flat 1, 2 …" west → east — the core (Lobby / Stair / Lift names) unpinned; printed names carry their flat (`PrintedRoom.flat`), `stats.flats` = each flat's name + printed area (only its own stamp); `pickByRooms` takes the taken rooms as rivals and no longer adds a seedless face whose doors all lead elsewhere; a lift read as no room's name names its face. `text.ts readTypes` reads the block around a printed flat area with no TYPE beside it (Type B found; a letter wins over a digit, "TYPE-8"). `core/flats.ts`: a pinned flat takes its printed area from its type label, pinned-only flats come west → east, and a group that is no flat by itself joins the ONE pinned flat its doors / low edge / zone line lead into (a room he closes later). `studio/review.ts`: the count is one line per flat ("Type A: 13 of the 14 room names read on it are closed and named — missing: Ver. Its closed rooms add up to 1795 sft; the printed flat area gives about 2408."), the room rows say which flat ("Type B: Ver is open …"), a printed name inside a lobby / lift / stair room is no longer counted closed (it was, on the whole floor: Type B's Toilet 2), `fewRooms`: a sheet that prints a bedroom is a flat sheet — the note says the walls may be thin lines, never "ground floor"; `issues.ts labelOf` (one line): Name it / Close it put the room into its printed flat. UI: the Auto-trace box has a **Whole floor** button beside "Point at a flat" (`StudioApp.tsx`, 5 lines: the button, `pickTrace(null)`, the hint; worker message `floor`). **Firefox as the founder (harness `E:/dev/tmp/s24/l4/l4.mjs`, logs `before.log` / `merged.log`, shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s24/l4/`; `Docs/AUTOTRACE_SCOREBOARD.md` "After Level 4"):** Whole floor — Sheltech L2: **Type A + Type B + core** (198 walls, 30 rooms, all labelled; "Type A: 13 of 14", "Type B: 11 of 13 — missing Toilet 2, Ver"; Check these 68, Issues 6); BTI 2nd: **Flat 1 / Flat 2 / Flat 3** (west small, the 2703 sft, east small) + Lift, Stretcher Lift (80 / 15); BTI 3rd: Flat 1 (south) + Flat 2 (north) + lift lobby, stair, stretcher lift (99 / 10). One flat — the same draft from all three spots on Sheltech A, Sheltech B, BTI north and BTI south; Sheltech B 12 rooms with Bed 2 in it from every spot (was 12 / 11 / 1 rooms); BTI south 121 walls, 16 of 21 names, kitchen / Bath-2 / study / help bed / its own PDR in, the north's Bath-2 out; BTI small west 6 of 7 (was 10 of 16 with the big flat's names), east all 5 (was 7 of 10); Banani: no "ground floor" message. Check these + Issues on the eight test flats 378 → 342 (rose: BTI south 53 → 62 with its 4 regained rooms, Banani 66 → 69 with 2 more rooms traced, BTI 2nd big flat 45 → 56 — see open). Node `solve.test.ts` NEW READER **75 of 116, unchanged**. Tests: `flats.test.ts` (one-flat pin, whole-floor grouping, a later room joins), `solve.test.ts` "Level 4" (a synthetic two-flat floor: whole floor = 2 flats + the lobby in none; three clicks in flat B, one beside the party wall where the old rival rule lost B's bed → one draft), `roomRows.test.ts` (per-flat count, rows, Name it's flat, the lift room, the note), `text.test.ts` readTypes (TRACE_OCR only). **Still open, and why:** BTI 2nd floor's big flat is now always picked from its dining's printed name, and from there the flood (capped by the printed area, breadth first) reaches the unnamed box between the lift lobby and its powder room — the old pick gave exactly this when clicked on that name (node: 128 walls, 48 / 9), 119 / 42 / 3 only from a spot 1.5 m west; flooding from all the flat's rooms at once was tried and was worse (133 walls) — reverted; the real fix is knowing where the core ends beyond the lobby's printed size. Rooms the tracer does not close stay open in their flat (Sheltech L2 B's Toilet 2 inside the lifts' face, BTI south's Bed-2 / Bath-3 / verandas). A flat with no printed type is "Flat N"; a whole flat drawn by hand beside a traced "Flat 1" is numbered "Flat 1" too and joins it (pin one room New flat). Banani: the click's flat is not found by seeds (6 px names), the trace picks a lift face into "Flat 1"; its walls are thin lines (out of scope). The second drawing on BTI 3rd floor is not traced by Whole floor. Not done: walking ONE flat alone in 3D (day 10).

**OPEN — the 3D view on a weak laptop (founder, 2026-10-13: "i couldn't run plotline on a lower configuration laptop. but on that very same laptop i used to play gta v … it was not usable in that laptop at all"; he had suggested drawing only what is in view, as maps and games do).** Not a lane yet (question, not an order). Facts measured 2026-10-13 on main: the viewer opens at quality HIGH unless the link carries `?quality=low` (MANUAL §2 lists it, he did not know); high = a GTAO ambient-occlusion pass (16 samples + denoise) on a 4× MSAA half-float buffer, at up to 2× the screen's pixels, a 2048 sun shadow map, max anisotropy; low = no AO, 1024 shadow, still MSAA + 2× pixels; frames are drawn continuously even when nothing moves; 71 PBR textures (69 at 1024², 2 at 2048²), 29 glTF models with no level of detail (the plants are 1.7–5.3 MB each); three's frustum culling already skips what is outside the view, so "load only what is in view" is not the missing piece — a flat fits in memory whole; what is missing is a cheap tier (fewer pixels, no AO, no MSAA, smaller textures, lower-detail models, no idle frames) and, likely, that a two-GPU laptop runs the browser on its integrated chip while GTA gets the game chip. Needed from him: the laptop's model / GPU, the browser, which view (walk / Studio preview), what "not usable" looked like (black, slow, crashed), and whether the live link with `?quality=low` runs. CLAUDE.md 2026-09-24 still says low-end machines are not a launch gate — his to revisit. **→ Answered 2026-10-18 by LITE (SESSION 27, "LITE DONE"): a Lite tier, a Quality menu for everyone, the device's pick marked.**

**Furniture selection — DONE (2026-10-13, Opus agent on worktree branch `worktree-agent-ad3ffdb2c4a4ec702`; MERGED on main e0cade2, 2026-10-13, pushed; 1046 tests + typecheck green on main).** FOUNDER CLARIFIED 2026-10-13 after the merge: his complaint was the 3D WALKTHROUGH ("i couldn't move or select a few inside the 3d walkthrough"), not the Studio. The 3D causes (the NaN planter leaves, glass taking the click, a 6 px drift = a click) are fixed and measured for SELECT and G (32 of 108 → 112 of 112; G 6 of 13 → 13 of 13). NOT separately measured: DRAGGING those pieces to a new spot in the walk view — the next session checks that first, by mouse in Firefox on his draft, before day 10. His complaint (2026-10-09): "i cannot select furnitures like sofa, cabinet, tv and etc whenever i want. there is an annoying glitch" — "sometimes selects sometimes doesn't". Checked by mouse in Firefox on HIS Banani Unit A draft with its sheet picture: Studio tool F, and Preview 3D → Edit furniture (dollhouse and walk) and G. **Problems found first (six):** (1) a click whose mouse slid 3 px or more counted as a drag — the piece moved a few inches, or (where that spot is refused) stayed in his hand, and his next click on another piece was a refused put-down (Studio and 3D); (2) Studio: a quick drag let go before the screen redrew left the piece in his hand although it fitted; (3) Studio: a wall cabinet, hood or cushions drawn over a piece selected the piece under them, every time; (4) Studio: a print or a wall TV is a 1-px line on the whole-plan view — almost impossible to hit; (5) 3D: a piece seen through a window or a glass door picked the glass — walk starts on his balcony looking through the sliding door at the bed: nothing selected, and G said "point at a piece"; (6) 3D: his greens planter has a 1-cm jog in its outline; the leaves hung over that zero-length side were broken numbers (NaN), and one broken leaf put the 3D's list of "what is under the mouse" in the wrong order — in the living a click on the sofa, TV, cushions or prints picked the wall behind them, in other rooms it happened to work. That was the "sometimes". **What changed:** a press that slides up to 6 px is a click — it selects, never moves — in tool F and in 3D; the Studio holds a dragged piece at once; tool F picks what the plan draws on top (a drag of cushions or a TV standing on its unit still moves the piece under them, as in 3D); a thin print / wall TV is picked within 6 px of its line; Edit furniture and G look through window / door glass and water (a buyer's comment still lands on the glass); a planter side of length 0 hangs no leaves. Commits 992290c, e9257f2, 13412fe + this docs commit; pins in `studio.test.ts` (pieceAt) and `engine.test.ts` (pickedAt through the slider glass; no NaN leaf in any planter of his draft). MANUAL §3 Edit furniture, §5 F, §10. **Before → after (same harness, his draft, Firefox):** Studio tool F — sofa / cabinets / TV / beds / wardrobes / prints / table / chairs at three zooms and right after edits (a drag, undo, R, V↔F, a wall dragged, a room named, Preview 3D and back), a hand's drift of 0 / 3 / 5 px: **33 of 92 → 92 of 92**; quick flicks released as they should: 7 of 12 → 12 of 12; every piece on the plan at the whole-plan view, drift 0 / 4 / 5 px: **59 / 3 / 13 of 68 → 68 / 68 / 68 of 68**. 3D Edit furniture — every piece in view in the dollhouse (as opened, zoomed) and walking (start on the balcony, living, dining, kitchen, master bed), clicked with 0 and 4 px drift: **32 of 108 → 112 of 112**, nothing moved, nothing left in the hand; **G** with Edit furniture off: **6 of 13 → 13 of 13**. Shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s24/fur/before/` and `after/`: `s1-*` = one click each on the sofa, TV and wall cabinet in tool F, and on the bed through the balcony glass, the sofa, TV and wall cabinet in 3D; `b2-*`, `a4-*` the runs. Logs + harness `E:/dev/tmp/s24/fur/` (a4, a1, b2, s1; `run.mjs <steps> /studio`). **How he checks it himself:** open his draft in `/studio`, press **F**, click the sofa, the TV, a wall cabinet over the counter, a print — each is named under Selection and does not move, even when the hand slides a little. Then **Preview 3D** → Enter: he starts on the balcony; **Edit furniture**, click the bed through the glass door → its name shows bottom left. **Rooms → living**: click the sofa, the TV, a cushion → each named. **Edit furniture** off, point at the sofa, press **G** → "Moving: 3-seat fabric sofa"; **Esc** puts it back. **Still open:** on the plan, a piece wholly under another — the hob + oven under its chimney hood, a ceiling light, pendant or wall AC over a bed or table (7 pieces on his draft) — cannot be clicked there: a click picks the piece drawn on top; pick it in 3D. A possible rule (his call): a second click on the same spot picks the piece under it.

**Free furniture moves — DONE (2026-10-13 night, Fable, on main; 1045 tests + typecheck green).** The founder, on the "A chair blocks the door" screenshot: "just let us change locations however we want for now as long as it doesn't overlap with another furniture or something. let us not worry about blocking passage and all." Done in `studio/furniture.ts whyNot` (one rule for the Studio's F tool and the 3D view): a moved / dropped piece is refused only outside its room, overlapping another piece, or a wall piece covering a window; the door / passage / entrance clear zones and the `outOfZones` nudge are gone from moves; the auto-furnish presets still lay out clear of doors. CLAUDE.md decision added, MANUAL §3 Edit furniture row updated, tests: studio.test (door zone + entrance now allowed), arrange.test (3D the same); the Sheltech Veranda 1 step-in-strip test removed with the rule. NOT yet checked by mouse in Firefox (the next session's first drag on his draft is the check). Superseded, kept for the record: **"A chair blocks the door" with no door near (founder, 2026-10-13 night, screenshot: dragging the dining table in his Banani draft, Studio tool F, the ghost red everywhere in the dining).** Reproduced in node on the fixture `founder-banani-unit-a-2026-10-09.json` (2026-10-13): the dining (3.5 × 5.0 m) has a 4.71 m wide PASSAGE on its whole east side (the open edge to the living) and a 1.76 m passage to the south; `furnish/presets.ts buildSides` treats every passage like a doorway whose leaf sweeps the room — a keep-clear strip `DOOR_CLEAR` = 1.0 m deep along its WHOLE width. So a 1 m strip along the open side plus 1 m at the south passage plus the entrance zone leave the 1.6 × 0.9 table with its six chairs exactly 2 of 204 grid spots (scan: 140 "Blocks the door", 14 "A chair blocks the door", 35 "Blocks the entrance"). The rule is right for a 0.9 m door and wrong for an open-plan edge. Not fixed (he asked what, not for a fix). Proposed rule for him to approve: an opening wider than ~1.5 m (a passage or a door that is a pair of leaves) keeps clear only a walking lane, 1 m wide × 1 m deep, at its middle — or nothing where both sides are the same flat's living / dining; the message should name the opening ("the open side to the living"). Same rule feeds the presets (`furnish`), so auto-furnishing of open-plan dinings is cramped by it too.
**Curtains — DONE (2026-10-14, Opus agent on worktree branch `lane-curtains`: 90ef367, c4984aa, 2dff53a, bd27705 + this docs commit; MERGED on main 67fdf20, 2026-10-14, pushed; 1052 tests (+7) + typecheck green on main).** The founder, 2026-10-13 night, on a screenshot of Bed 2 of his Banani draft in the walk view, looking up at the corner window: "look at the curtains. they are goddamn hilarious. empty, all over the place, cannot move or delete. they need to be separate items as well as being connected with windows. no weird gaps like this". **Problems found first** (Firefox, his draft with its sheet, Preview 3D, main 12d1bfc; shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s24/cur/before/`): (1) each curtain was two strips about 8 cm wide hanging INSIDE the window, far apart — when another opening stood next to the window (Bed 2: the small window on one side of the sliding door, the glass strip on the other; Master bed: the glass wall and the glass panel either side of its sliding door) the rod was pulled back inside the window and the panels shrank to their 8 cm minimum: that was the "empty" look and the bare glass and wall between strip and window edge; (2) Bed 2's small window and sliding door each had a rod, ending finial to finial, and the sliding door's rod ran on past it over the glass strip; Master bed's ran into the corner by the glass wall; (3) they were no furniture piece: in Edit furniture a click named nothing, Del did nothing, G said "Point the mouse at a piece of furniture" — the only control was a Curtains tick box in the window's panel (Edit openings, Studio O); (4) the Studio's F did not show them at all. The living's windows on his draft are glass walls: no curtains there, before or after (the rule is unchanged). **What changed:** curtains are a furniture piece, "Curtains, linen (1.94 m wide)", hung ON their window (`FurniturePlacement.openingId`, + `shiftM` when slid — two optional fields, nothing else in src/core). Every layout hangs them from their window again (`furnish/presets.ts hangCurtains`, run by `layoutFor` and the presets): as wide as the window plus up to 15 cm past each side — less where a side wall is nearer (never round a corner), half way to the next opening on that wall (two windows side by side: two rods end to end), or another pair at a corner — floor to ceiling, one pair per window the old rule dressed (bed / living / dining / study window or sliding door onto open air; never a glass wall). Move or resize the window (Studio, or Edit openings in 3D) and they follow; delete it and they go; older drafts' `curtains: false` still hangs none. They look like curtains now (`procedural.ts curtains`): two ivory linen panels gathered in deep folds on rings, one stacked at each side over the window's edge and frame (about 36 % of the glass in all, what the daylight bake already counted), a dark steel rod with ball finials on two wall brackets. In 3D Edit furniture and Studio F (one rule, `studio/furniture.ts`): a click picks them, a drag or G slides them along their own wall only, in 3" steps, with the hand (they stay their window's: it still moves and sizes them), Del deletes (one Ctrl+Z brings them back; a deleted pair stays deleted), Add → Bedroom or Living → Curtains hangs a new pair on the window pointed at, sized to it (any window, a glass wall too). They stop only other curtains: a sofa may stand against a curtained window, and they are never "covering the window". The Curtains tick boxes are gone (Delete is the off switch). Edit furniture → click a curtain panel (its name shows bottom left) → drag it sideways → Del → Ctrl+Z. Core `validate`: a wall end inside a column's block is no `dangling-vertex` (the "Loose wall end" mark is gone there). A dragged column never pulls to itself (`exclude`). `HARD` (vertex / wall / wall face / column) = the kinds a dragged wall or a stretched end takes whole. Seen in Firefox as the founder (`E:/dev/tmp/s22/user/snap1.mjs`, shots `E:/dev/plotline-shots/s22/user/`): with a column dragged on Sheltech A, the Wall tool reads "snapped: column" 4 px short of its side and "snapped: wall face" beside a thick wall, the drawn wall ends on the column and only its free start carries a mark. ## ▶ START HERE — SESSION 22 TASKS (written 2026-10-06 at the end of session 21; the founder reviewed the day's work, found five things and set the order below; he will open a new session). Branch `claude/brave-ritchie-ypmrbd`, 960 tests green, NOT pushed, NOT deployed.

**His five findings (with screenshots in his session; analysis below is mine, agreed with him):**
1. **Walls do not connect to columns.** `src/studio/snap.ts` knows only corners and wall centre lines; a column is no snap target, so a wall drawn to a column stops short (crack in 3D, "wall end in the open" mark).
2. **Only the centre line and the two ends of a wall are sticky.** The two FACE lines (centre ± thickness / 2) must snap too, weaker than the centre line (status line: "snapped: wall face") — a new wall butting against the side of a thick wall.
3. **"The measurements are very wrong (1401 vs 2408 sft)"** — two things: (a) the review message "The rooms add up to 1401 sft; the printed 2736 sft flat should give about 2408" counts CLOSED rooms only; in his trace about half the rooms were still open (Space 3, the bed beside it, the lobby side), so the number is right about what it sees but the wording is wrong — say "closed rooms add up to…" and point at the open ones; (b) the real error is rows like "Bed 1: drawn 13'-1" × 17'-3", printed 13'-0" × 15'-6"": auto-trace put a wall 1'-9" off (the far face of a thick wall, or a neighbouring line). **His exported draft is IN THE REPO: `src/data/fixtures/founder-autotrace-type-a-2026-10-06.json`** (Sheltech Level 2 = `public/assets/plan-sheltech-l2.jpg`, his fit 26.864 px/m at 491.3 / 139.75; 102 walls, 84 corners, 17 labels, 12 with printed sizes — Dining, Kitchen, Bed 1 13'-0" × 15'-6", Bedroom 2, Living, toilets, verandas; area 2736). Start task 3 by loading it in the Studio with that sheet, comparing each drawn room against its printed size (review.ts sizeCheck), and finding which line the tracer took for the off walls. Keep it as a fixture test like `founder-sheltech-a-draft.json` ([[acceptance-is-founder-draft-in-3d]]).
4. **Curtains on some windows, not others.** `details.ts curtainSides`: only kind `window` (never `slider`), only in bed / living / dining / study, only when the other side is open air (`OPEN_AIR` = balcony, shaft, or outdoors). The bare one in Bed 3 is a slider or faces the veranda (kind `veranda` is not in OPEN_AIR). Fix: windows AND sliding windows alike in those rooms; veranda / balcony count as outside; plus a per-window switch (curtains on / off, later the fabric) under "every element changeable".
5. **Room-by-room finishes still missing** = step 3 of his goal (MANUAL §11 top). Finish slots are per unit by room GROUP today; needs a choice per room (and per wall), a Finishes panel that works per room, saved per flat in the project. Plan first (touches `src/core` finish slots), then one Opus agent, then a user-persona run + MANUAL.

**Decision on "which rooms belong to which flat" (2026-10-06):** NO "Flat" box to type — he called it extra work and he is right. Derive flats from the drawing: rooms connected by DOORS form one flat; the lift lobby / stairs / shafts (common core) are shared, so a flat stops there; the printed "TYPE-A ±2736 SFT" label inside the cluster names it and gives its area for the check; the Studio tints each flat's outline and names it, the floor list shows "Floor 2 — Type A, Type B"; if the guess is wrong he clicks one room and moves it to the other flat. Nothing to do when the guess is right.

**The order (his, approved):**
1. Snapping: columns (corners + faces as snap points; a wall ending on a column is joined there — no open-end mark, no crack in 3D) and wall faces (findings 1 + 2). `snap.ts` + tests; a user-persona run. **And (founder, 2026-10-06): make the magnets 30 % LESS magnetic** — the snap reach (`SNAP_PX` in StudioApp, 10 screen px today) goes down by 30 % for everything, the new face / column magnets weaker still than the centre line.
2. Curtains (finding 4) + the clearer wording of the area message (finding 3a). Small.
3. Measurements (finding 3b): on his exported draft find why walls land on the wrong line, fix the tracer, keep the file as a test. Waits for the file.
4. Room finishes (finding 5): plan → build → user-persona run → MANUAL.
5. Flats inside a whole-floor drawing, derived as decided above (per-flat finishes need it).

**Standing rules:** acceptance = an agent acting as him (sheet image, mouse, Firefox, no JSON / code) before reporting; problems listed before fixes; plain words; MANUAL updated in the same commit; HANDOFF after every merge. Harness for user runs: `E:/dev/tmp/s21/user/run.mjs` (copy it, new OUT / TMP / profile). Tokens were tight (his week resets ~2026-10-10): few agents, small steps.

7. **The Studio must be able to make all of it** (his rule: what Claude's units show must work on any unit he traces): the new wall choice (keep free-standing), the wall height field (exists), the zone kinds in the room-kind picker, a level field on a label, a hand-drawn column tool (stage 2 found there is none). **THE FOUNDER ANSWERED the three questions (2026-10-04 evening) — do not ask again:**
1. **All the projects, not one.** "Why not do all of them? Choose any one first as you like, but doing more means better implementation." Order (manager's pick): `Sheltech/` (already the demo tower) → `Sheltech Banani/` → `Sheltech dmd/`. Every level of each: basements, ground, rooftop.
2. **THE BENCHMARK RULE (binding, his words):** "whatever you are going to do, make sure the changes are generalised — it must not look like that because you edited in there manually; we are building a benchmark and what you show is the minimum in the benchmark." So: the hand-authored levels are DATA ONLY (walls, openings, labels, zone kinds, levels, placements) — every bit of how they look comes from general rules keyed on kinds and geometry (never a unit id, a project name, a room name spelled one way, or geometry typed into `src/three`); the same result must come out of a level the founder traces in the Studio with the same data. Proof required: (a) a test that no `src/three` / `src/furnish` code references a level's id or name; (b) one level re-made by driving the real Studio (Firefox) and compared to its hand-authored twin in 3D — same zones, same finishes, same furnishing; (c) three projects with different shapes all coming out right from the SAME rules — a rule that only fits one sheet is not done.
3. **Buyers must be able to WALK the ground floor, the rooftop and the basements** ("yeah buyers should be able to walk around, later if not now") — in scope; build it after the levels render right in the Building view, and if the session runs out, say it is not done and put it first in the next one.
4. **Furnish the common levels — this UN-PARKS the per-level furnishing rules:** lobby / reception (desk, seating), rooftop (benches, loungers, pergola-type shade if a CC0 one exists), GYM equipment (Banani's ground floor has a gym), POOL (dmd's roof: water, deck, loungers), community room, children's play area, guard room, drivers' waiting. Presets by zone / room KIND like the flats' presets (`src/furnish`), same staff tools (move / turn / delete / add from the library). Poly Haven CC0 first; where it has no realistic piece (gym machines, play equipment), a code-built piece in the same photoreal materials or leave the zone empty and SAY so — never a low-poly or cartoon stand-in (CLAUDE.md Assets).
5. **Greenery is a real feature, for flats too:** lawns and BIG trees on the levels; and planters as a choice — "make those options available inside apartments as well where we can choose what planters we have inside an apartment balcony; if not now we are doing them later". Build: a greenery group in the furniture library (planter boxes, potted plants, small trees — per size) usable on any balcony / veranda / roof / lawn through the existing Edit furniture → Add; a balcony's preset picks a default planter set; whether buyers (not only staff) may choose a balcony planter set as a finish-like option with a BDT delta is NOT decided — default: staff only this session, note it for him.

6. **No costs / prices on greenery or planters** ("don't worry about any costs right now, that is a feature for much much later") — planter sets are a staff choice with no BDT delta; do not build buyer-priced planter options.

Everything the five hand-authored units show must work on any unit he traces in the Studio. "Mistakes are not what I am looking for, or sloppy work."** So: root cause first, verify every item in the real app (Studio draft → Preview 3D → shots looked at by the manager) before reporting, his draft fixture `src/data/fixtures/founder-sheltech-a-draft.json` is the test unit beside type-a.

**The 9 asks, what was found (read-only, 2026-10-04), what to build, how to accept:**
1. **"Small blips whenever I move my view"** (shot: Bed-2, type-b/c floor 3 — speckle along the wall foot bottom right, a thin sun streak on the wall, a tiny brown upright at the wall foot). Not measured yet. Candidates, in the order to test: (a) GTAO noise — `render.ts:126–130`, 16 samples, screen-space, so the pattern re-rolls as the camera moves (the speckle patch); (b) sun shadow map — 2048 px over the whole flat, PCF radius 2, bias −0.0005 (`render.ts:98–103`): edges crawl when the view moves; (c) z-fight where the 12 mm skirting / wall-end faces are coplanar; (d) the brown upright = probably a sliver of skirting (`probe.mjs` the pixel). Method: add debug switches (`?ao=0`, `?shadows=0`) so each cause can be turned off alone, sweep the camera on a REAL GPU (headed Edge, not swiftshader — it does not show GPU-only noise), diff consecutive frames, fix what measures; the founder confirms on his PC with the same switches. Accept: a slow pan across Bed-2 and two other rooms, frame diffs show no flicker outside moving edges.
2. **"We have the drawings of whole projects (basement, ground, rooftop). I cannot duplicate types and create the whole building."** Today the Building view is hard-coded: `src/data/building/{demo,sheltech}-tower.ts`, `towerOf(unit)` matches by unit id → a Studio draft has no Building view at all. This UN-PARKS "level templates" (CLAUDE.md, parked 2026-09-26) — he asked for it. Build in stages, stop after any stage that is green: **stage 1** a Project made in the Studio = a list of floors, each floor = flats (traced unit + offset + mirror), "repeat this floor on floors N–M", saved in this browser like drafts; `towerOf` reads it → Building view works for his own units. **stage 2** ground / basement / rooftop as traced levels shown as shells (walls, slabs, no furnishing rules). **stage 3 (not this run)** per-level furnishing rules. Touches `src/core` types (a Project / Tower type: extend, never change; PLAN FIRST per CLAUDE.md). Accept: his draft duplicated on 6 floors + a mirrored neighbour shows in Building view; the two built-in towers render unchanged (building.test.ts).
3. **"Preview 3D of my Studio work has no Finishes."** ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED: Studio drafts and auto-trace write `finishSlots: []` (`studio/model.ts:148`, `:1425`, `trace/solve.ts:1016`); the five hand-authored JSONs each carry 7 slots (4 floor groups, 2 wall groups, 1 ceiling; options with BDT deltas). Fix: ONE shared default catalog built from room kinds (living/dining/bed → floor group, wet rooms, verandas, kitchen; walls; ceiling), applied wherever a unit has no slots (viewer load, `/u/preview`, `/s/<token>`), with stable slot + option ids (invariant 4) so a buyer's choice survives a re-publish; the five JSONs should end up using the same catalog. Accept: `/u/preview` on his draft shows Finishes, every option changes the right rooms, thresholds still appear only between different floor groups.
4. **"The partition option adds an ugly little thing on the floor"** (shot: Dining & family living ↔ Living, the 4.66 m passage). ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED (script `scratchpad/passage-check.ts`, vite-node): the skirting runs straight ACROSS every passage — 2 runs through the middle of each of type-a's and sheltech-a's passages (threshold = false there, so it is not the stone strip). `three/details.ts:60` leaves passages out of `cuts`; the reveal runs (`:86–99`) already turn the tile round the jamb. **A session-17 regression (691bf77).** Fix: cut the face runs at passages; add the test that failed to exist ("no skirting run crosses a passage span", on type-a + the fixture). Accept: his exact view, no kerb; the tile still wraps both jambs.
5. **"The partition here has a nice white contrast; when I did it manually I did not get it — generalise."** Every `passage` already gets the white casing in code (`three/openings.ts:136`), so the gap is in how his partition was made. Hand-authored passages are 2.7 m high on 3.0 m walls; the Studio's Passage (O, key 4) defaults to 4' × 7'. NOT reproduced — first step: make a partition the way he would (Passage on a wall; a passage as wide as its wall; two walls with a gap and no opening) and see which loses the frame. Likely fixes: a passage whose casing legs fall off the wall ends still gets a head + jamb lining; a doorless gap between two wall ends in line gets the same cased opening; Studio passage default height = the hand-authored 2.7 m (or wall height − 0.3). **Founder's answer 2026-10-04: the partition in his screenshot is one Claude traced (type-a / type-c's 4.66 m passage); he only edited it a little. He did not say how his own manual partition was made — so cover both cases (Passage tool on a wall; a gap between two wall ends).**
6. **"GTA / map apps only render what is in view — apply it if it helps."** three.js already skips objects outside the view (frustum culling is on by default). What it does not do is skip rooms that are in front of the camera but hidden behind walls. Do NOT build blind: measure first (`renderer.info` calls / triangles, frame time per room, on his PC class). Cheap safe wins before any room culling: the sun shadow map re-renders every frame though nothing moves (`shadowMap.autoUpdate = false`, update on sun / furniture change); 10 forward lights. Room-by-room hiding (portals) only if a measured room is slow — it can cause pop-in, which is exactly the "blips" he hates. Report numbers either way.
7. **"To move a piece I must press Esc, select it, move it; I want one button that picks it up and it moves with me."** Today Arrange turns pointer lock off (`PlotlineScene.ts:408`, grab-the-room look). Build: staff-only, in Walk with the mouse still locked — a key (G) picks up the piece under the crosshair, it follows the crosshair's floor point as he walks / looks, same rules as Arrange (1 ft grid, wall snap, no overlaps / blocked doors, red when refused), R turns, click or G drops, Esc puts it back; saved to the same `plotline.layout.<unit>`. Inside the founder's arranging exception (move). Buyer links never get it.
8. **"The developer side with the list of comments — finish the pipeline and options; Supabase holds the data."** Build everything except the paste: migration #2 (append-only `events` rows: comment / finish selection, keyed by share token; RLS on, no policies; RPCs `add_event(token, …)` for buyers and `events_for_staff(key, …)` for staff, same staff-key pattern as share links), the viewer on `/s/<token>` writes comments + selections through it (local fallback when offline), a staff page (Studio → "Change list", or `/staff`) listing per link: who / when / object / comment / chosen finishes with BDT deltas, newest first, superseded rows greyed (invariant 6: never update in place), export CSV. Test the SQL in a throwaway local Postgres if one can be had at $0 (PGlite as a dev-only test dependency) + mocked-fetch tests; the live check waits for his paste (extend `verify-share.mjs`). **Never touch the connector's project `txbt…`.**
9. **"Issues in the floor plan do not lead me to the problem; some are not worth it. I want colour-coded marks at the exact points and a visual suggestion of exactly what to change."** Today: a text list; a click pans + selects (`StudioApp.tsx:1181`), rows without ids do nothing (`model.ts:1281–1315`). Build: every issue drawn ON the plan at its point all the time (red = breaks the 3D: loose end, crossing walls, opening past its wall; amber = worth a look; grey / hidden = cosmetic, e.g. an unnamed 1 m² space), numbered to match the list; hover / click a row pulses its mark; each mark shows the fix as a ghost — a loose end: dashed line to the wall it should reach + "Fix" (uses `reachEnds` / close-gap); crossing walls: the crossing point + "End it here"; unnamed room: the room tinted + type the name in place; one click applies, one Ctrl+Z undoes. Also the "Check these" review rows get the same marks. `core.validate` signature unchanged (extend only). Accept: on his fixture every issue is findable without reading the list, and each "Fix" either closes the issue or is not offered.
10. **"Moving furniture is inconvenient: where it does not fit, the game should not dismiss it and put it back. Like Clash of Clans — red lines and a warning, but I can still move it, turn it, and place it where it fits."** Today a refused drop reverts the piece (viewer `arrange` drop → the overlap / blocked-door rules; `PlotlineScene.ts:916–934` sends `drop`, the reducer in `viewer/arrange.ts` + `studio/furniture.ts` refuses). Build: a piece in hand follows the pointer EVERYWHERE (grid + wall snap still apply); where the rules refuse it, it turns red with the reason ("overlaps the bed", "blocks the door") and stays in hand; R turns it while red; a click on a red spot does not drop and does not revert; it drops only where it is valid; Esc returns it to where it was picked up. Same in the Studio's tool F and in ask 7's carry. The rules themselves do not change (CLAUDE.md: refuse overlaps / blocked doors) — only that an invalid position is shown instead of thrown away. Tests: the reducer keeps an invalid held position and never commits it.
11. **"I should be able to remove a window, door or partition from inside the walk view, change their size and move them. The edit modes should be floor plan, dollhouse and inside walk. I do not want to go to the floor plan and start rendering from zero to see the result."** NEW FOUNDER DECISION (2026-10-04): staff may edit OPENINGS in 3D (walk + dollhouse), not only furniture — add it to CLAUDE.md's Decisions with the feature's first commit. Build: staff-only "Edit openings" beside Edit furniture: click an opening → handles on its two ends (drag = resize, the Studio's rules: min 0.3 m, snap flush to wall ends / neighbours, red + refused over another opening), drag the body = slide along its wall, Del = remove, a small panel for kind / width / height / sill in feet-inches; one undo per gesture. Reuse the Studio reducer's `update-opening` / `drag-opening` / `resize-opening` / `delete` (`studio/model.ts:1010–1117`; model.ts already ships in the viewer's shared chunk) — one source of rules, no second implementation. "Not from zero": the camera stays where it is and only what changed is rebuilt — the wall, its two rooms' skirting, the opening's joinery, the daylight of the rooms it touches (`setUnit` today rebuilds everything, `PlotlineScene.ts:251–278`; measure a full in-place rebuild first — if it is under ~300 ms with the camera kept, that is enough). Moving WALLS in 3D is NOT in this ask. Wall graph stays the single source of truth (invariant 1): a door edit shows in both rooms.
12. **"While walking I want a minimap, bottom right or so, showing exactly where I am."** Build: a small plan (room outlines from `deriveRooms`, the current room tinted, its name), a dot + a view cone for position and facing, north as the unit's `northDeg`; bottom right above the HUD's edge, clear of the sun pill; M toggles it; walk mode only (the dollhouse is already a map). For everyone, buyers too (it is orientation, not editing). SVG over the canvas, updated from the camera each frame without a React re-render per frame. Not asked, not built: click a room on it to jump (the Rooms list does that).

## ▶ Session 16 (2026-10-03): WAVE 21 MERGED = item 0 done (precision joins, opening tool, flat preview); 594 tests, build green, worktrees removed, pushed; NOT deployed (founder: `npx vercel --prod --yes`)

**Solver output unchanged this wave** (C proved the eval tables + drafts byte-identical): product 77 / 116, oracle 82, Sheltech wallRec 64 / 68 %, BTI 58 / 88 / 79 %. Everything below is Studio-side.

**A MERGED 45388aa — hand-fix precision (594 tests, 7 new; shots `E:\dev\plotline-shots\s16\precision\`, script `E:\dev\tmp\s16\precision\verify.mjs`):**
- **(a) In-line snap** (`snap.ts`, Snap kind `'in line'`): a dropped end within min(tolM, 3 cm) of a wall's centre LINE past its end lands on that line (priority corner > wall body > in line > axis align; axis lines keep their dashed guide, diagonals get none); a chain drawn on from a wall's end follows that wall's line instead of the 45° rounding. Nudge is deliberately NOT snapped (a 1" step would never leave the 3 cm radius). **Extra fix:** `alignX` / `alignY` took the FIRST corner within tolerance, not the nearest — at default Sheltech zoom the 10 px radius is ~37 cm, so a neighbour a few cm off could win and leave a 179° corner: the likely source of the founder's wedge. Now nearest.
- **(b) Heal after joins:** `settle()` wraps every join site (drag-end, nudge, chainAdd, join-walls → also load / auto-trace): a degree-2 corner within 1.5° of one line and ≤ 2 cm off the line through the far ends is straightened; same thickness + height → the two walls merge into one (`healStraight`, openings keep their plan position via `rebase()`, reversed pieces mirror hinge / swing); different widths → only straightened. The whole-unit pass heals only BENT corners, so the five hand traces load unchanged. Healed corners drop out of chain / selection.
- **(c) Wall drawn over another** (`dropOverlaid`, first in settle): same height, within 5° of parallel, the shorter's ends within the thinner's half-thickness of the longer's line and inside its end blocks, ≥ half overlapping → the shorter is removed, its openings move onto the longer where they fit; toast "Removed a wall drawn over another". A 1.1 m railing beside a full wall is never removed.
- **(d) Door on a stub** (`fixStubs`, last in settle + after set-wall-length): a DOOR wider than its wall − 5 cm or on a wall < 0.6 m moves onto the wall continuing straight through either corner at the same plan position (clamped, refused over another opening), else removed with toast "Door wider than its wall — removed; redraw it on the long wall". Windows / sliders / passages only when wider than the whole wall (the literal rule would delete Sheltech A's 0.35 m toilet vent on its 0.53 m wall at load).
- Browser: Sheltech A loads unchanged (84 walls); a drop 1.5 cm off the Dining east wall's line reads "snapped: in line", lands on x = 9.487, the two pieces become one 7'-0" wall (84 → 83); Preview 3D shows one wall `v_de_t1n → v_de_ds`, no corner (`b-healed-3d-walk-Dining.png`). Side effects: merging walls can move preset furniture in rooms with no saved layout (the Dining shelf moved); bent corners in the founder's saved draft heal when he presses Join walls or touches them; a hair-long hidden connector `joinOverlaps` already created is not removed by (a)–(d); `arrange-*` chunk +3.8 kB raw (model.ts' reducer sits in the chunk the viewer shares — moving the reducer / `isUnit` / `normalizeUnit` apart would make the viewer chunk stable).

**Try it (founder, after deploy):** `/studio?unit=sheltech-a` → V → drag a wall's end handle to just off a collinear wall's line → it snaps and the two become one; Issues → "Join walls" heals bent corners in an old draft. `/studio` → O → panel "Place an opening" (or keys 1–4) → hover a wall → one click. Select an opening → drag its end ring. `/studio` → Choose plan image (Sheltech Level 2) → Auto-trace → wait for "Point at a flat…" → hover Type-A / Type-B → click → instant draft.

**NEXT (in order):** (1) founder deploys + tries the three above on his hand-fixed Sheltech draft (export its JSON for us if the 3D still shows a wedge); (2) **planter / veranda strip outer lines** (faces.ts: strips > 20 m² / fill < 60 % spanning both flats → split by the flats' outer walls, each half a planter box; the 12–16 wall-recall points on Sheltech); (3) **lever 5 reader** (AI montage on the founder's Gemini key + "type this size" rows; Sheltech "Space N" rooms); (4) pillars in 3D + Studio pillar snapping; (5) eval truth: core rooms out or a "without core" column; (6) **BUILD_PLAN check: day 10 of 42, code ~55–60 % of the 6-week scope, 0 of 3 discovery demos, Supabase 0 lines** — manager's recommendation: demos this week on BTI type-b (88 % walls), next engineering wave = Supabase (orgs / units / share_links, RLS, signed links), auto-trace levers only after a demo says tracing speed is the objection.

- *What exists to build on:* `traceTracks()` in `src/trace/tracks.ts` returns `{ tracks, blocks, glass, plantMask, classes, gaps }` (every axis wall as an interval on a track; columns as blocks; blue glass pixels; plant mask; undecided gaps). `src/trace/rooms.ts` returns per fitted room edge the stretches `wall / window / door / thin / open / unsure` with exact start/stop; `deriveWallsFromRooms` emits `unwalled` stretches. `src/trace/merge.ts` (`roomsOnTracks`, `closeCorners`, `pickByRooms`) already turns a one-sided thin line into a 1.1 m low wall (`WallSeg.heightM` → `Wall.heightM`, convention from the hand traces: railing 1.1 m, planter edge 0.45 m) and plant green beyond a face into a planter edge, each with a review item. `src/trace/hints.ts` has `planterMask`, `findHints` (fixture symbols) and `findStairs`.
- *Which rooms:* AODs (14; small shafts, thin-line or light-grey walls, 161–215 grey, label "A O D" / "AOD" with no size), planters (9; green fill / foliage; label SUNSHADE/PLANTER or none), lift cores (3; drawn as one block or hollow), stair (1), passage (1), Sheltech-b dining (open plan), verandas (railing + planter edge + a slider to the room), Sheltech toilets/bedrooms/lobby/foyer (low-res doors, double doors read as nothing, glazing lines blurred).

(c) **AOD / shaft faces:** closed thin-line faces ≤ 4 m² with an AOD/shaft/duct label or no label and no fixture → kind `other`, name from the label, no door (flag). (d) **lift / stair:** a block or hollow rectangle with a LIFT/STAIR label → one face, walls = the drawn ones; `findStairs` for unlabelled flights. (e) **double doors + low-res doors (Sheltech):** two mirrored quarter arcs = one door of the gap's width; at < 30 px/m let the room edge's `door` stretch decide a gap even when the tracker's ring test fails, if the edge evidence is strong; report false doors. Per room class before/after (the integration report's classes: no printed size 29 / fitted-not-closed 24 / fit wrong 7 / pick missed 4).
- *Rules that still bind:* a wall starts/stops where the ink does; never bridge an undecided gap; positive evidence only; everything added by lever 2 is a low wall / planter edge / flagged face, never a silent full wall; room kind from the printed label, hints only flagged.

The wall stage's door/window guesses count as walls for the "ends on a wall" test (`wallsPlus`). Touching window pieces on one wall (a slider's leaf line splits the gap) count as one run for the ≥ 1.2 m veranda slider rule. Tried and reverted (both cost spill on BTI C): letting the flood walk through a veranda's wide window run; adding a labelled veranda whose wide glazed side is on the flood's edge.
- **Solver (9f23e7f + follow-up), raw eval 42 → 44 of 116** (Sheltech A 8, B 8, BTI A 10, B 7, C 9 → 11; spill A 20 / B 3 / C 0 %): a glazed side running from where one crosswise wall STOPS to where another does (a room's whole window side, corner to corner) is a window too — a stop is an end no other wall or door/window guess carries on (door jambs are not stops, else a wardrobe rail between two jambs became a window); second-pass lines must be ≥ 0.8 m (short tilted bits of a veranda's edge line took a window guess's free end so its wall never joined); a guess carrying the wall line on counts as the stub (the next room's window on the same outer wall past a partition's T); a second-pass line starts at the stub's end. Tried and reverted: band extents from two-row coverage instead of the median row (swapped BTI C Kitchen / B Bath-3 in for C Help bed / Bed-3 out, +6 % spill); letting a small labelled face behind a wide window run join the flat (Sheltech A spill 20 → 29 %, K. veranda still out).
- **Tried, no gain:** a sub-pixel ink-mass wall width for the Sheltech scale (+10.8 %) came out worse (grey walls, JPEG, 3.4 px) — the scale there is an input-resolution problem (300 dpi brochure), not a measurement one.

**WALL TRACKER (founder's ask, 355dabe) — the default wall stage now.** "Walls get crooked when thick; the draft should be exactly as thick as drawn; follow the wall from the middle." `src/trace/bands.ts`: on an axis-aligned sheet a wall is a run of ink whose cross-section is the same short run column after column — for every column, every vertical ink run of a wall's width (2·rCore … 2·half·blobFrac px) marks its middle pixel as a centre carrying that width (sub-pixel: the run's two edge pixels count by their coverage between the paper 2–3 px out and the wall's own grey; nothing outside the run counts — JPEG halo); runs of centre marks along a row are horizontal walls (a gap is bridged when the row stays inked across it for at most one wall width: a crossing wall's body or a column — a longer thick stretch is the next wall with its own centre), straight by construction, thickness = the median width; ends run on through inked corner blocks up to one wall width (the graph stage nodes the overshoot); a block as long as it is thick is no wall; vertical walls the same with rows and columns swapped. `traceWalls({ tracker: 'bands' })` runs it after `wallSkeleton`, keeps only the skeleton pieces no band covers ≥ 70 % (angled walls, arcs — `coveredByBands`), then `tidy` + `findOpenings` as before. The draft carries the measured thickness to the nearest ½" (`toUnitGraph(segs, exactTh)`) instead of the 5" / 10" classes. Studio: the "Band tracker (exact wall thickness)" checkbox in the Check-these section (localStorage `plotline.tracker` = 'skeleton' opts out); the stats line says "band tracker". Eval switch: `TRACE_TRACKER=skeleton` on solve.test / walls.test compares the old path.
- **Raw eval 44 → 45 of 116** (Sheltech A 8 → 6, B 8, BTI A 10, B 7 → 10, C 11); spill A 8 / B 3 / A 0 / B 1 / C 6 %; cover A 29 / B 46 / A 41 / B 34 / C 42 %. **Wall-level eval (walls.test, centre lines within 0.15 m): precision 70/76/70/77/83 % (was 62/76/69/76/82), recall 62/61/49/71/62 % (was 54/57/46/67/60), thickness bias ≤ 0.03 m** — every sheet up. Scale on Sheltech stays +10.8 % (thicknessScale's 0.25 px bins straddle 3.5 / 4 px; a paper-reference variant measured +3.5 % but scored 42 rooms — the 4 rooms it lost were worth more; kept the honest one). Sheltech A's 2 lost rooms are the low-res sheet's thin stubs — the same 26 px/m ceiling as before.
- **Tried inside the tracker:** bridging inked gaps up to 1.5 wall widths (a 5" band ran on along a 10" wall, off-centre — Bath-1 open); counting halo pixels outside the run (+18 % on Sheltech, the area rule then locked the scale in at +22 %).

**FOUNDER 2026-09-30 (after trying the band tracker on Sheltech B in the Studio): "this is all wrong … I just wanted walls and doors. Why are you adding windows and partitions?" → `KNOBS.windows = false`: the wall stage's window guesses, both glazing passes and the blue-glass pass are OFF. The draft = the flat's walls (band tracker, exact thickness) + door arcs. Rooms form only where walls and doors close them: raw eval 45 → 13 of 116 BY DESIGN (every room behind a window stays open, a review item). Also: every wall within 1 m of the flooded floor is kept (not only the walls a flooded cell touches), and the flood never enters a closed lobby / lift / stair face (it used to walk through the lobby into the next flat's foyer). Overlays `scratch/shots/wd/` looked like walls + doors on BTI A and Sheltech B. The window rules (wave 18's glazing work) are all still there behind `KNOBS.windows` / the closer — do not turn them back on without the founder.

Nothing to do there until the founder decides on thin-line boundaries. **Banani** walls are 2–5 px black lines at ~20 px/m (columns black, lift core dark grey ~70): resolution, not threshold — the 300 dpi brochure is the lever, the PDF he added is the same 1000 px raster.

**Remaining causes at 44 (same diagnosis tool):** thin-line boundaries ~20 + open-plan passages ~11 (founder scope, unchanged); foliage over planter walls 12; split by an extra wall ~8; window/slider gaps still unbridged ~7 (BTI B Bath-3 / Living, C Kitchen / Study / Bed-1: the gap's pane lines start away from any stub); light-grey walls 4; BTI B K. veranda + H. toilet "face built, not picked" (the K. veranda's slider is read as two windows, the flood stops there, and its perimeter share on the flat is 25 %).

Founder wants to check things himself — told to deploy the current Studio if he wants to try it (harmless, Studio-only) and to look at the overlays.

**Founder's wall-tracing workflow (session 12, BINDING, overrides the older "stop at furniture / text" tracker wording; sent to the wave-17 solver agent):**
1. **Text first:** read room names + all printed text, erase the glyph boxes.
2. **Then plant sections:** mark planter / green / sunshade strips (label, green fill, foliage) before tracing walls; they bound the flat, not rooms to grow into.
3. **Then start in the middle:** from the click, find the nearest thick wall lines and follow them and every branch outward until nothing is left.
4. **Ignore what isn't a wall or a door, and resume:** anything in a thick wall's way (furniture, fixture, text, hatch, dimension line, a column changing thickness) is skipped. Keep going along the wall's direction; when the thick line comes back on the same alignment it is the SAME wall and tracing resumes. Door arc in a gap = door; window drawn in a gap = window.

**Open small items:** the Studio worker does not send the colour image (`AutoTraceOpts.rgb`) → colour propagation (Banani fills) is off in the app; size-mismatch rows don't re-check after edits.

**Founder decisions 2026-09-27 (02:00), binding:**
- **Furniture move — one-time exception to the CLAUDE.md rule "no drag-and-drop furniture placement editor in the first 6 weeks".** The rule stays in CLAUDE.md and still applies in general; the founder explicitly allows ONE thing: a grid-snapped move/rotate of existing pieces inside the Studio (his words: "like Clash of Clans, the field is divided into squares"). Spec: 1 ft grid, snap to walls, 90° rotation, the drop is refused when it overlaps another piece or blocks a door swing / entry clear zone, per-unit overrides saved in the unit JSON `furniture` array (presets stay the default layout), Studio only, never the buyer view. Nothing beyond that (no free placement, no catalog UI yet) without asking him again.
- **Exterior plaster gets a texture** (render agent's finding: study panes look flat because the outside wall is a flat colour) — changes every exterior face + the dollhouse; accepted.
- **Small wet rooms (< 3 m²) may pitch down up to 25°** (was 10° cap) so the WC enters the frame; larger baths keep 10°.
- **Green areas, two rules:** a green strip on the ground floor or rooftop = turf (lawn + a few shrubs); a green strip at storey height (sunshade planter, veranda planter) = raised bed with trailing plants over the outer edge. Never trees / big pots. Planters hidden from the Rooms list.
- **"Half a point per wave is slow" — answer given:** each wave fixed one room class; the average over ~19 rooms moves little, and every frame hits the same ceiling (flat repeated neighbour facades, blown bath tiles, untextured exterior plaster). So wave 11 leads with the GLOBAL look (lifts all 70 frames at once, worth ~1 point), adds a **hero-rooms score** (living, dining, kitchen, master bed, master bath, verandas) beside the full mean, and gives humans the grid move for the last 10 %. Honest ceiling for real-time WebGL + CC0 assets ≈ 7.5–8; beyond that = WebGPU path tracing later (see "Founder questions 2026-09-27" below).

**Scoring honesty (founder 02:25: "make sure the score doesn't hallucinate and bump up just because I asked for improvements"):** the score is produced by a separate blind agent that is never told what changed, which wave is which beyond "baseline / candidate", or what result anyone wants; its prompt must not contain words like "improved", "fixed" or the fix list; it rescores the baseline images itself (so a stricter director lowers both columns, and only the delta counts); every row carries a one-line reason naming what is visible; the manager never edits a score. If a frame is a capture fault (wrong room, mid-flight), the fix is to RE-SHOOT and re-score that frame with evidence kept (`recheck\`), never to strike the row silently — the b-bed-2 case above is documented that way. Report row means and the headline; state the director-to-director rescale when it happens. A wave with no visible change must be reported as "same", and it is fine to report a drop.

**Neighbour buildings (founder 02:25): do not spend effort on them.** A simple texture is fine, none is fine too; they never gate a wave. All tokens, effort and intelligence go into the project's own building and flats. (This overrides the director's fix #5 "veranda backdrop" — accept verandas at 5 for now.)

### Wave 8 result — manager score (Fable), 2026-09-26 ~01:00, 52 shots at 15:30 in `E:\dev\plotline-shots\wave8\score\` (a-/b-/c-)
- **Type A 6.5 / Type B 6.5 / Type C 6.5** (wave 7: 6.5 / 6.0). Flat number, different shape: furnished rooms are clearly better (living, bedrooms, study, dining, verandas now 7–7.5: clean glass, no blown tile, even walls, oak wardrobes, varied art, believable neighbours, sun patch on the Type B living floor seen from the veranda); small door-view rooms are worse (baths, powder, help room, closet, Type A kitchen: 3–5).
- **Regression to fix first (wave 9 #1): the door view for baths/tiny rooms.** Standing 0.5 m inside the door looking down 20° fills the frame with the near wall + floor and cuts the fittings (`a-bath-1`, `a-powder-room`, `b-bath-3`, `b-help-room`). Tried an 8° pitch (`score-pitch8\`): worse, the fittings leave the frame entirely. So it is not the pitch: the stand point must be the far corner / diagonal that sees the most fittings from the greatest distance (the wave-7 `b-bath-3` frame), with the door view only as fallback when no corner is ≥ 1.5 m from the vanity. Help rooms: the cot sits at the frame's bottom edge; aim at the cot's centre from the door at eye level, or accept that a 1.5 m room can't be framed and show it in the dollhouse only.
- Other wave-9 items seen in the shots: a wall AC looms in the top corner of `a-entry` and `c-entry` (the AC belongs to the dining room while the entry stand point is in the passage; the 2.2 m rule only checks the stand point's own room); Type C entry has the wooden cube shelf 0.5 m to the right of the stand point; `c-veranda-living-` faces the wall corner with the lounge chair in the foreground; `a-kitchen` stands 0.5 m from the cabinets (Type B's kitchen frame is the model); oak wardrobes fill a third of `b-bed-2`, `a-bed-3`, `c-bed-1` (stand point should keep 1 m from a tall piece in frame); the ceiling fan fills the top of `a-living-room` / `c-living-room` (minor); sliders with invisible glass read as open (a faint sky reflection would fix it).
- Typec's rule-break list (planters get a lounge chair, L-shaped Bath-3 gets no shower, curtains hang on the study's interior glass partition, the two-zone rule skips the irregular dining) is still open.

## Open work

Parked by founder decision (PC first, VR later): #1 desktop canvas black when WebXR enabled, #10 VR window glass opaque.
These are the first two VR tasks when VR resumes. VR draw calls 2.5–3.1k/eye: fine tethered, too many for standalone Quest.

### Founder questions answered 2026-09-27 ~01:30 (Session 8)
- **"Green areas = plants that flow down?"** Two rules (see decisions in START HERE): turf on ground/roof strips, trailing-plant raised beds on storey-height planters.
- **"Make furniture selectable/movable first, or polish first?"** Movable first, constrained: a bed blocking a doorway is a 10-second fix for a developer with a grid move and an endless chase with rules; move + select share the foundation the finish catalog and "swap this piece" need; polish has no finish line and the score loop keeps running anyway. → the one-time exception above. Oversize furniture is a separate cheap rule fix (size by room size).
- **"Nvidia renders a few pixels and AI fills the rest — can we?"** That is DLSS: a neural upscaler + frame generation in the GPU driver, local, free per frame, native games only — not available to a browser app. What applies to us later, on high-end PCs: (a) WebGPU path tracing with a denoiser (three.js has a WebGPU renderer and an experimental path tracer) — the realistic route to "more reality" in 1–2 years at no per-view cost; (b) generative img2img over a rendered frame + depth map — excellent for STILL brochure renders (cents each, seconds), poor for the live walkthrough (30 fps, frame-to-frame inconsistency, cloud GPU $1–3/hour per viewer). Plan under the $0 rule: keep the walkthrough real-time, add path tracing when WebGPU is common, sell AI-enhanced stills as a paid export (Phase B/C).
- **"Could we license Plotline to luxury hotels / Airbnb to show room tiers and views?"** (02:40) — parked, not a build target. Hotels fit the engine (one plan, hundreds of identical rooms, tiers, views by floor/time) but their rooms already exist, and a photo or a $10/month 360 scan beats a render for anything that exists; our edge is what does not exist yet. Carry-over cases worth a pilot AFTER developers pay: hotels under construction/renovation selling before opening (several in Dhaka), per-room view simulation with the sun slider, configurable/connecting rooms. If it happens it is per-property white-label licensing of the engine, a packaging job. Airbnb: no (millions of hosts with phone photos, the platform won't license). Do not spend effort on it before paying developers.
- **"Auto-update failed: claude.exe in use"** — harmless; after closing every session (incl. VS Code) run `claude update`, then `claude doctor`, then `claude --continue` in `E:\dev\Plotline`.

### Founder questions answered 2026-09-25 (they set the order below)
- **"Is this a system or one polished plan?"** The pipeline is rule-based (walls, furniture presets, materials, lights, room views work on any traced plan), BUT every rule was only ever judged on Type A. Until a second, different plan goes through untouched and scores close to Type A, "system" is unproven. **This is now the top priority** — no more Type-A-only polish before it.
- **"When can I see it?"** Now: the live link. Founder is testing it himself and will send a bug list. Comments/notes save only in the founder's own browser (Phase 0).
- **"Make every object selectable/swappable (table, sofa, fan, AC, lights, curtains, glass colour, door, mirror, shower handle…) — foundation for a catalog."** Added below. Today: furniture pieces, curtains, doors/windows (as a whole), walls/floors/ceilings are already clickable with their own ID; walls/floors/ceilings already swap via the Finishes panel. Missing: ceiling lights (not clickable), parts inside a piece (mirror inside the vanity, shower handle inside the shower, window glass, door handle), an AC model (none in the kit), and a catalog of alternatives per object.

## Founder's first tracing session — 2026-09-26 ~08:40 (live Studio, a small tilted-wall plan; timer 13:15) — THE adoption test, passed

He traced a 5-room plan with a 45° wall himself, placed doors and windows, labelled rooms, previewed in 3D, in **13 min 15 s** (masterplan target: 15 min on the second attempt). "Somewhat happy… the wall lines help tremendously to place windows and doors; the walls fell nicely on my tilted wall." His caveats, verbatim intent (also in PRODUCT_SPEC §2.8):
1. **"I cannot move the walls anywhere."** The V (Select) tool does drag corners, walls and openings (StudioApp `drag`), and the panel edits X/Y — he never found it. Discoverability bug: the hint only says "click to select, drag to move" once V is active. Also wanted: nudge the selection with arrow keys (1", Shift = 1').
2. **"Show me how wide it is going to be before I place it"** — Clash-of-Clans-style ghost footprint. Walls: the ghost wall exists (draw.ts draws it at `chain.thicknessM`) but 5" at fit zoom reads as a line — draw the ghost as the filled true-thickness slab with its length label. Openings: **no ghost at all today** — the O tool creates a 3'-0" door centred on the click with no preview. Wanted: hovering a wall shows the full-width outline (door leaf + swing arc / window triple line) sliding along the wall before the click.
3. **Openings anchor from an edge, not the middle:** today `add-opening` centres the opening on the click; he wants to place it from one side (e.g. the jamb against a corner). Design: the ghost snaps its nearer EDGE to a wall end / adjacent opening within 10 px, else centres; the "From corner A" field stays for exact numbers.
4. **A wall sticking out at a corner in 3D** (`Image #11`, far corner past the tilted wall): the "Corner is not joined to anything" warning in his Issues list — one corner sits a little off. **Founder: do NOT make snapping stickier; keep it as it is.** He just needed to shorten that wall or move the corner a bit, which is item 1 (moving + nudge keys). Only change: the issue text says "click to find it".
5. Folder move (his, uncommitted at the time): `Demo drawings/Btibd/img_1..5.webp` (the old demo tower, developer BTI) and **`Demo drawings/Sheltech/`** (a NEW developer: Basement 1–2, Ground floor, Level 1–6, Rooftop; each level = Type A + Type B (L/O), ±2736 sft each, lobby + 2 lifts + stair core). **This is the third-developer system test the plan has been waiting for.**
6. Level templates (ground floor, basements, typical floors, rooftop — rooftops and verandas have furniture, basements don't): **parked by the founder until the living floors are done.**

### Next, in order (rewritten after the founder's session, 2026-09-26 ~09:00)
1. **Founder deploys** (`npx vercel --prod`) and walks `/`, `/u/type-b`, `/u/type-c` and `/studio`; his bug list goes first. Wave 9 = commits 8a00305..49e7ee1 (+ HANDOFF).
2. **Wave 10 — Studio ergonomics from the founder's session (items 1–4 above), one Opus agent on `src/studio/`:** ghost footprints for walls (true thickness, filled) and openings (full width, leaf/swing or triple line, sliding on the hovered wall); opening edge-snap to wall ends and neighbouring openings; arrow-key nudge (1" / Shift 1') for the selection; Select-tool hint visible in every tool ("V: move") and a one-time toast after the first closed loop; issue rows say "click to find it"; **snapping radius/behaviour unchanged (founder's explicit wish)**. Tests in studio.test.ts for the reducer parts (edge snap, nudge). Acceptance: the founder re-traces the same plan and hits no caveat from the list.
3. **Wave 10 second half — Sheltech Level 2 Type A traced in the Studio** (by the founder, timed, or by an agent if he prefers) and run through the pipeline untouched: the real system test. Score it blind next to A/B/C.
4. **Wave 11 #1 — a frame-coverage test in spawn.ts.** Every remaining bad small-room view (powder rooms, H. toilet, a-bath-3, b-bath-1, c-bath-3, b-bath-3's door leaf, the closets, a/c-bed-3, c-bed-1's wardrobe, help rooms) is "something flat fills a third of the frame", which distance rules cannot see. Project each candidate's view (65° FOV, 16:9) onto a coarse grid, estimate the frame share of door leaves (ajar 20°), wardrobes/tall pieces and the nearest wall, and reject > 15 %. Then: wet rooms from the doorway at eye height ≤ 10° down aiming between basin and toilet; help rooms lengthwise from the cot's foot; closets from the opposite wall. Pure, testable on A/B/C.
3. **Wave 10 #2 — the two glass leftovers:** `shower_screen` panel in procedural.ts gets the same sky-envMap glass as the windows (today a milky veil in a/c-bath-2); find and remove the "dashed outline" on the study↔dining partition window seen from the verandas; small study/bath panes blow out to white in direct sun at 15:30 (a-study, c-study) — check the pane's opacity vs the sun.
4. **Wave 10 #3 — furnish:** the two-zone lounge chair must not sit beside the coffee table on the dining side (c-entry: its back hides the table — prefer the side away from the table, B's chair may move); a cot with a visible mattress and pillow (reads as a white plank); the closet rail unit seen from the door is a close-up — a shallower unit or the second unit on the far wall.
5. **Founder request 2026-09-26 — the whole building.** Stack the traced floor plates (floors 2–8) with slabs, a ground floor plate from img_5 (parking, lobby core, driveway) and a rooftop from img_1 (garden, stair head, tanks); a "Building" view beside the dollhouse, orbit from outside, only the current flat furnished, other floors as shells (walls, windows, slabs — draw-call budget), current flat highlighted; replaces the floating dollhouse and grounds the neighbour blocks. **Founder decision 2026-09-26:** no drawings exist for floors 4/6/8 — use the 3rd/5th/7th plan (Type B upper flat + Type C lower flat) for floors 3–8; floor 2 = img_3 (Type A, one flat); ground = img_5; rooftop = img_1; basement img_4 optional (below grade, skip in the first version). Full version later: ground/rooftop traced in the Studio as levels, floor picker, sun on the whole tower.
6. **Third plan from ANOTHER developer** is still the real system test (Type C turned out to be Type A's own layout on floors 3/5/7). Founder sends 2–3 brochure plans from other Dhaka developers.
7. Studio follow-ups from the first round trip: small-room labels overlap at fit zoom; timer reads 00:00 after an import; Type A's own drift vs the drawing (Bath-3 is L-shaped, closet wall 0.14 m off, PDR depth) — retrace Type A in the Studio as the founder's first tracing session (masterplan week 2) and diff it against the JSON.
